(2) **G-ident on Linux** — linuxdeploy sets a `RUNPATH` with patchelf on every dynamically linked ELF under the AppImage's `usr/bin` (read in its source by the review, not measured), so `package-linux.sh` reports a bundled ≠ staged digest as a warning with the RUNPATH `readelf` finds and holds `libexec/` == staged strictly; macOS and Windows hold bundled == staged strictly (macOS assuming an unsigned bundle). The owner's first Linux packaging run measures it. (3) **P14** — the Stage 2 callers named in its *Add* sentence are not characterised: `infer::get` has exactly two call sites (`routes/files_types.rs`, `security/sanitizer/mod.rs`), both pinned, and the pointer read, Open and `send` take the row's MIME type or the file name, so a verdict change alters what an upload accepts, never what a row holds. (4) **Q01** — the "captured real file" is a literal built by the writer's own serializer and round-tripped; a daemon-written `discovery.json` joins it as a second literal at the next isolated live check (E4/E5). (5) **P02** — `bunx tauri info`, its gate's second half, was not run (it may query registries); the first half — `cargo metadata --locked` from `src-tauri/` resolving the root lock's `tauri 2.11.5` — stands; not established. - A **DU** is an owner decision. Rev 3 asks nothing in prose: every open choice is a DU with options, a recommendation and what changes under each answer (§4). Nothing that adds an enforcement point, widens a component's job or reverses an accepted design is adopted here.
- "Eligible" means at least 7 days old on the day the branch is cut (rev 2.1 §5.1 item 5, one rule for both ecosystems): a crates.io `created_at` or npm `time` date, UTC, at or before cut − 7 days. On 2026-10-10 that is 2026-10-03.

The storage design's Stages 0–2 landed (`931460a4..a9dbed4e`, `b8b768b6^..44d53783`, `cfebde4c..d673478e`); L8 is reported (`tasks/memory-redesign/10-learning-evaluation.md` rev 2.2, final) and slice 4's packaged verification is recorded (`08-evaluation-report.md` §4.1 at `8fde2925`) — rev 2.1's DU-1 prerequisites are history.
- The dependency wave has **not** landed: Rust 1.93.0 (`rust-toolchain.toml:2`), `rust-version = "1.93"` (`Cargo.toml:32`), rusqlite 0.38.0, sha2 0.10.9, aes-gcm 0.10.3, keyring 3.6.3, rmcp 0.16.0, tauri 2.11.5, Vite 7.3.6, Vitest 4.1.11, root `bun.lock` `lockfileVersion: 1` [C].
- Of the 68 direct Rust dependencies, **33** have a newer release (17 need a semver-incompatible requirement change, opener 0.9 among them for the first time; 16 move with the lock), plus `serde_yaml`, whose replacement is a choice [M-today: `SP/reports/direct-deps-2026-10-10.tsv`, the harness's crates.io table]. 15 JS packages are behind, two of them majors (Vite 8, Vitest 5), plus the three `@tauri-apps/*` packages — 18 `bun outdated` rows [M-today: gui A]; the lock's tauri 2.11.5 still pairs with `@tauri-apps/api` 2.11.1, and the latest crates are a minor ahead of both.

**The one stated assumption.** The owner asked for every package at its latest major and for "more flexible" apps. Rev 3 reads *flexible* as the 10-10 audit's G1–G8: a thin Tauri 2 shell over the daemon (G1 extraction, G2 build provenance, G3 HostBridge and a compatibility contract), a browser-first React dev loop, low memory, fast startup and fast compilation, measured before promised (G4–G8). That reading is Part II (§7). **One sentence from the owner replaces it**, and Part II then follows the sentence; Part I (the dependency wave, §5) does not depend on it.

**The four things that matter most.**

1. **Nothing measured loses data, and the measurement is now on this tree.** The formats that move — AES-GCM secrets, SHA-256 names, the SQLite file, TOML and YAML — were differential-tested in September [M-sep]; today the whole wave compiles and passes the current suite on `7295f91b` [M-today]. What is still **unwritten**: P05's AES byte-format fixture and the one missing literal (the project-UUID partition key `21a5e10e73692d40`), and P14's extraction/sniffing characterisations — so 5,081/0/16 says nothing about DU-6/8/9 (no existing test reaches them, by grep). And the archived E6/E8 tooling (replay driver, comparators, SQLite probe) **cannot be used as gates**: the replay is incomplete and never fails, the comparators write files and differ in source, the probe lacks a module and embeds schema 45 / cache 3 [M-today: reconcile R12–R14]. §6 rebuilds them.
2. **Five advisories are open in today's lock and the wave clears all five.** lopdf 0.34.0 (a crafted PDF aborts the daemon — reachable), rustls 0.23.43 (reachable), quick-xml 0.31.0 ×2 (likely reachable), rmcp 0.16.0 (server transport, not compiled). Nothing in Part II and no freshness waiting may delay P04, P15 and P16; DU-1 (C)'s security slice stays available.
3. **The Tauri Rust/JS pair moves first, in one commit** (P31a, Phase B since rev 3.9). The Tauri CLI cannot see the JS packages under Bun, CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri`, and the Rust requirements were broad, so P31a — right after P03 — moves both locks to the 2.12 pair, pins `tauri-plugin-dialog` exactly to its JS twin and narrows `tauri`/`tauri-build` to the minor; P04 is then a plain refresh with no Tauri hold. Rev 3.1–3.8's ten-crate hold at P04 (measured: the four-crate form does not compile, the ten-crate form does [M-today, rev 3.1]) existed only so P04 could stay lock-only while the JS half waited for Phase J — a sequencing convenience the owner's rule of 2026-10-12 removes. P31a names what 2.12 brings: windows 0.62 drops Windows 7; wry 0.57 / tao 0.37; the GHSA-w28w-mhc8-qvjv fix (`tauri <= 2.11.5` affected).
4. **Part II is separate, reviewable work with no measured promise.** The shell can leave `openalpaca_storage` (≈1,900 lines moved, 7 crates leave, `cargo tree` says so) and gain a HostBridge, a compatibility contract and honest build provenance; every RAM/startup/compile claim is **[NE]** until the §7.1 baseline exists, and dependency comparisons are always old-vs-new on the same architecture.

## 2. Findings refreshed by area

Only what changed or was corrected since rev 2.1. A September finding that still stands is referenced (`plan.md:NNN`), not repeated. Where a rev 2.1 statement is now simply false, the reconcile ledger's Part 4 (`SP/reports/reconcile.md`) lists it; the ones that matter are folded in below.

### 2.1 Data safety

A claim is load-bearing here when being wrong about it could lose or orphan data or secrets, silently change an on-disk or wire format, or change what people see.

**Stored secrets: AES-GCM (`llm.toml` `secret_encrypted`) and the installed-process-key contract.**
- The construction and byte format are unchanged since September: AES-256-GCM, random 12-byte nonce prefixed, 16-byte tag, no AAD, `aes256:` + base64 STANDARD, the raw 32-byte key from 64 hex [C: `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/key_encryption/mod.rs` — `PREFIX` `:11`, `BASE64` `:7`, `from_hex` `:140`, `ensure_at` `:161`, `encrypt` `:243-260`, `decrypt` `:263-285`]. The two commits since `5e5ac317` (F3: `0047cf90`, `33feb142`) touch only `:6-101`, `:110-118`, `:125-138` [M-today: `git diff` hunk ranges]. So September's cross-decryption evidence (10,000 plaintexts × 20 keys both ways; ring 0.17.14 oracle, 18,030 checks) still describes this code [M-sep], and the archived AES hunks apply unchanged [M-today: `git apply --check --include='…/key_encryption/*' secrets.diff` exits 0; applied in the re-cut].
- **The precedence prose in rev 2.1 (`plan.md:208`, "`OPENALPACA_MASTER_KEY` overrides it") is wrong for the daemon.** At boot the daemon reads or ensures `state/.master_key`, installs it as `openalpaca_llm`'s process-local key and **removes** `OPENALPACA_MASTER_KEY` from its environment, inherited or not [C: `apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:155-170`, the call at `:160`; `key_encryption/mod.rs:64-75` `install_process_key`, `:86-92` `install_process_key_and_clear_env`]; the library reads the installed key first, then the environment, then the file [C: `:130-138` `from_env_opt`]; the CLI uses the library order through `load_or_generate_at` / `load_at` [C: `:99`, `:116`]. The archived migration does not undo this (its hunks are elsewhere).
- **P11's gate therefore widens** (R22): after the bump run the 13 `keys::key_encryption::tests::*`, `tests/master_key_leaves_the_environment.rs:41` (`a_child_spawned_after_the_install_sees_no_master_key_and_the_installed_key_still_decrypts`), `load_at_never_mints_a_master_key` (`key_encryption/tests.rs:213`) and the CLI state-key tests (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ai_config_helpers/tests.rs:36-68` [A08]). **Hashes that name files (sha2 0.11): eleven sites, and the project-UUID partition keys.**

**MCP protocol behaviour (rmcp 0.16.0 → 3.5.0).**

- **The protocol version our `initialize` offers changes again at 3.5.0.** 3.4.1 offers `2025-11-25`; **3.5.0 moved `ProtocolVersion::LATEST` to `2026-07-28`** [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/model.rs:177` vs `rmcp-3.4.1/src/model.rs:175`] and added `LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE = 2025-11-25` (`:202`), `NO_INITIALIZE = 2026-07-28` and `has_initialize()`. `ProtocolVersion::default()` is still `LATEST` (`model.rs:157-159`), `ClientConfig::default()` uses it (`:1439-1447`, `:1198`), `ClientHandler::get_info` returns `ClientConfig::default()` (`handler/client.rs:277-279`), our `NotifyingHandler` has **no `get_info` override** [C: `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:68`] and starts with `handler.serve(transport)` [C: `:665-666`] → `legacy_startup` sends the version unclamped (`service/client.rs:693-705`, `890-915`). **Consequence: under 3.5.x our `initialize` offers `2026-07-28`, a revision whose own spec has no `initialize`.** How real servers answer is [NE]. The re-cut's measured state carries **no override** [M-today]; DU-5's sub-question (d) — pin the offered version through a `get_info` override returning `ClientConfig { protocol_version: ProtocolVersion::LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE, ..Default::default() }` — becomes a **required decision** for any 3.5.x (DU-25). The `MCP-Protocol-Version` header follows the **server's** negotiated `InitializeResult.protocol_version` (`streamable_http_client.rs:112-129`), not the offer, so the pin does not change the header [D] — rev 2.1's Appendix B #24, header half, answered.

- All eight steps combine with no semantic conflict on `7295f91b` [M-today]: 28 files changed outside the lock (+261 / −234: `Cargo.toml`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, two crate manifests, 11 sha2 sites, the AES module and its tests, 14 rmcp files, 3 serde-saphyr files); the lock +1,446 / −1,050, **732 → 764 packages**, 187 moved / 68 added / 30 removed HEAD → final. Patches: `SP/patches/recut-combined.diff` (183,083 B, lock included), `recut-source-only.diff` (45,876 B), per-step locks `recut-lock-{00-head,01-toolchain,02-update,03-storage,04a-secrets-prefix,04-secrets,05-documents,06a-rmcp-prefix,06-rmcp,07-web,08a-config-prefix,08-config,99-final}.lock` — today's reference results, replacing September's `combined-commits/NN-*.diff`; **they were cut without P04's Tauri hold**, so G-base compares with rev 3.1's `recut-lock-02-update-held.lock` at step 2 and `recut-lock-99-final-held.lock` at the end (§5.1 item 6), and the un-held series is the reference for steps 3–8's own moves.
- Duplicates (`cargo tree -d --workspace -e normal`): 101 top-level entries / 46 names / 84 name@version / 33 multi-version names → **109 / 52 / 93 / 37**. No longer duplicated (9): `infer`, `nix`, `foldhash`, `constant_time_eq`, `thiserror`/`thiserror-impl`, `toml`, `toml_datetime`, `winnow` [M-today: `a06`/`c05`]. Duplicates cost compile time, not correctness (rev 2.1).
- The final `cargo update` notes 22 dependencies "behind latest" before the wave and 12 after the rmcp pin — the held majors; a post-wave `cargo update --dry-run` was not run [NE]. E3 therefore gates on matrix conformance, not on "locks 0".
- The order of §5.3 is the order the re-cut took (toolchain → update → storage → secrets → documents → rmcp → web → config), with the format pins (P05, P14) and the CI/hygiene commits placed as rev 2.1 placed them.

### 3.2 Not forced by the bump: left out unless a DU says otherwise

- Feature trims: base64 `default-features = false, features = ["std"]` (not measured); rmcp `default-features = false` (P23, DU-5 — Cargo 1.99 also lets an edition-2024 member override an inherited dependency's `default-features`, so a per-crate trim of `zip` or `rmcp` needs no workspace change [Docs]); sysinfo with only `system`.
- *Rev 3.9: taken by P10* — the eleven forced digest rewrites call one `openalpaca_storage::hash::sha256_hex`, and the duplicated helpers go with them.
- `Atomic::<u64>::fetch_update` at `session_log/mod.rs:519` — the one rustc deprecation 1.99.0 adds — is replaced by the infallible `Atomic::update` (1.95; rev 3.9, not `try_update`); **not** in P03 (so the lint delta stays attributable); P36's (Required since rev 3.9; `modernization-plan.md` M04).
- Pinning Node (runner 22.23.3 and local 24.15.0 satisfy every floor).
- A `.cargo/config.toml` `registry.global-min-publish-age` — once the pin is ≥ 1.100 (§9).

### 3.3 Found in passing: pre-existing problems, separate work

Unchanged from rev 2.1 §3.3 and re-verified as still present [M-today: `git log`, C]: the MCP HTTP redirect leak (`transport/http.rs`, no redirect policy — one line, recommended soon as its own `fix:`); the unbounded reconnect ladder (`client.rs:325`); `client_info` never sent (no `get_info` override; rev 3.9's `Auto` lifecycle needs none, so nothing forces it — a two-line addition to P19 if the owner wants our identity sent); `openalpaca config set|unset daemon.*` drops comments (`daemon_config_cli/mod.rs:408`, `:438`); the Linux-only unused imports at `notification/imessage.rs:4`; README's Linux prerequisites lacking OpenSSL headers until P28; two rustls crypto backends in one binary (rev 3.9: P41 takes ours to one, aws-lc-rs; `ring` stays only on teloxide 0.17's reqwest 0.12 path); credential discovery reading the real macOS keychain from an isolated daemon (§5.2 switches it off).

New, or sharpened today:
- **`prepare-sidecar.ts` and tauri-build write and read the same path.** tauri-build copies `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` to `<target_dir>/<profile>/openalpacad` [D: `tauri-build-2.6.3/src/lib.rs:56-75` `copy_binaries`, called at `:535-551`; `tauri-utils-2.10.1/src/resources.rs:46-61`], which is exactly the "source binary" `prepare-sidecar.ts:43` compares by mtime (`:46-52`) and copies (`:81-82`); after any shell compile the shortcut compares the staged copy with a copy of itself. In the checkout: `target/release/openalpacad` absent; `target/debug/openalpacad` 153,165,936 B (Oct 10); the staged `openalpacad-aarch64-apple-darwin` 58,041,424 B (Oct 3; release or debug [NE]) [M-today: `ls`]. Rev 2.1 recorded the mtime rule; the loop with tauri-build is new. Q02 fixes it; until then E5's hand sequence applies.
- **The daemon in `libexec/` and the sidecar are different binaries from the same source**: the release scripts build `-p openalpaca -p openalpacad` jointly (`package-macos.sh:55`, `package-linux.sh:48`, `package-windows.ps1:58`) and the CLI enables reqwest `multipart` (`apps/openalpaca/Cargo.toml:20`), while the sidecar hook builds `openalpacad` alone (`prepare-sidecar.ts:60-61`) [C; the two feature graphs measured by A09]. Q03; until then E5/E7 build the daemon alone, once.
- **The MCP stdio-stub flake** did not occur in today's two full runs [M-today]; rev 2.1's "3 of 6 runs" stays a historical observation [NE].
- **T21** (pending, rev 2.1 §0): the shell reuses a live daemon past the token's 24 h expiry (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:40-49`) while `get_connection_info` refuses it (`:31`) [C]; Part II's compatibility contract pins today's answer as current behaviour and names T21 (DU-29).
- **`discovery.json` has no byte pin** (§2.1); Q01 adds one before anything moves.

### 3.4 Ordering with other planned work

- The paused "LLM providers as a table" design (`tasks/llm-simplify/design.md`, rev 1, paused 2026-09-23) overlaps P11, P12 and P29; **land this wave first**, as rev 2.1 said. Its keychain-naming change, whenever it comes, re-runs DU-10.
- Part II (§7) is ordered against Part I in §7.7.

## 4. Owner decisions

Each decision is recorded, once taken, as an *Owner decisions taken* row in `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0 (its eleventh such subsection; ten exist at rev 3.25 [C] — rev 3 wrote 3.23) with the commit that applies it, like MD-n and T-n. Nothing here is adopted by default: the shipped code is the "no change" answer, and a DU whose recommendation changed says why.

**Crosswalk.** `SP/reports/architect.md` numbers its proposals DU-23…DU-28; rev 3 renumbers them so the dependency-wave decisions keep the lower numbers: architect DU-23 → **DU-27**, DU-24 → **DU-28**, DU-25 → **DU-29**, DU-26 → **DU-30**, DU-28 → **DU-31**, DU-27 → **DU-32**. The archived report keeps its own numbers.

### 4.2 DU-1, DU-2 and DU-13 — the changed recommendations

- Facts [M-today; C]: L8 is final (10 rev 2.2) and slice 4's packaged verification is recorded (08 §4.1 at `8fde2925`), so rev 2.1's two prerequisites are met; draft PR #34 is open against `main` [M: `gh pr view 34 --json state,isDraft,baseRefName,headRefName,headRefOid` on 2026-10-10 — OPEN, draft, base `main`, head `feat/markdown-memory` at `f7235e7b`; an authenticated read that left the machine, disclosed; rev 3 had it from CLAUDE.md], `origin/feat/markdown-memory` is at `f7235e7b` (2026-10-02) and HEAD `7295f91b` is unpushed; `main` carries neither schema 4 nor Stage 2 (the pointer-row code, the five annotation helpers, the eleven hash sites exist only on this branch), and every audit and every measurement today was on this branch; the five RustSec vulnerabilities are open in today's lock.

**DU-13 — Bun version and lockfile format.** (recommendation changed)
- Facts [M-today: gui §2.1; Docs]: Bun 1.4.2 keeps an existing v1 lock at v1 on re-save (11 advisories remain) and writes v2 from scratch (0 advisories; four gates green); dependabot-core `main` bundles Bun 1.4.2 and accepts `lockfileVersion` ≤ 3 since 2026-10-06; the hosted updater's rollout is [NE]; the owner's Bun is 1.3.13 and must not be touched by the research; setup-bun reads `packageManager` from the **root** `package.json`; the lock is the root `bun.lock`.

Every local Bun must then be ≥ 1.4; the owner installs 1.4.2 exactly (§5.1 item 2). **Condition:** the first Dependabot bun run on the v2 lock must succeed; if it fails with an unsupported-lockfile error, fall back to (b).

Keeps Dependabot happy on any image. **Prerequisite: a Bun 1.3.x still installed** (§5.1 item 2 keeps the owner's 1.3.13 until P35's first bun PR succeeds).
- **(c)** Pin 1.4.3 — 1.0 d old, unmeasured; its install-path and TLS fixes are a one-line follow-up after 2026-10-17.
- Node: unpinned (every floor met); pin 24 via setup-node only for local/CI parity.
- **Recommendation: (a).** Under (b) P31 is authored with a Bun 1.3.x and the §9 checklist watches the lock version; the `grep lockfileVersion: 1` assertion idea is dropped. Under (c) re-run the four gates under 1.4.3 first.

### 4.3 New decisions for Part I

**DU-23 — The freshness collisions (the 7-day rule on the day of the cut).**
- Facts: §1.6's table — on 2026-10-10 the absolute latest of 17 packages is younger than 7 days; **today's groups measured the absolute-latest set** (recut: uuid 1.28.0, serde_json 1.0.152, toml 1.1.8, toml_edit 0.25.17, tokio-util 0.7.20, signal-hook 0.4.5 in the lock; gui: vite 8.3.4, marked 18.1.0, prettier 3.9.10, jsdom 30.1.2, @types/node 26.6.5, plugin-react 6.1.2, the 2.12.3/2.8.2/2.12.2 Tauri packages) — and the eligible set is a set nobody ran. Every one of the measured versions is eligible on or after **2026-10-17** (vite 8.3.4 from 10-15; marked 18.1.0, plugin-react 6.1.2 from 10-12; jsdom 30.1.2, signal-hook 0.4.5 from 10-11; toml 1.1.8, toml_edit 0.25.17, tokio-util 0.7.20, @types/node 26.6.5 from 10-16; everything dated 10-10 from 10-17). Two measured versions stay below the latest by choice: rmcp 3.5.0 (not 3.5.1, DU-25) and Bun 1.4.2 (not 1.4.3, DU-13). P04's refresh at the cut will move whatever appears meanwhile; "measured = taken" is a statement about today's names, checked by P04's gate against `recut-02-lockdiff.txt`. The five open advisories do not wait for this: DU-1 (C) is the counterweight, and the delay under (a) is seven days.
- **(a)** Cut on or after 2026-10-17 and take the measured set as the eligible set. No gate re-run beyond the ordinary ones.
- **(b)** Cut earlier and take §1.6's "newest eligible" column: P04 steps back with `cargo update -p <spec> --precise <version>` — `uuid --precise 1.27.0`, `serde_json --precise 1.0.151`, **`toml@1.1.8 --precise 1.1.6`**, **`toml_edit@0.25.17 --precise 0.25.15`**, `tokio-util --precise 0.7.19`, `signal-hook --precise 0.4.4` (the two `name@locked-version` spellings because the lock holds three `toml` and three `toml_edit`; the bare `-p toml` / `-p toml_edit` are refused as ambiguous, exit 101, and change nothing — all six steps measured, their lock `recut-lock-02-update-stepback-b.lock`, 6 moved [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-02a…h`]; the rule: any crate the day's lock holds at more than one version is spelled `name@locked`); the Tauri set held anyway; P31–P33 pin vite 8.3.2, marked 18.0.14, prettier 3.9.9, jsdom 30.1.1, @types/node 26.6.4, plugin-react 6.1.1; P31a takes the 2.12.1 pair — and **re-runs the four bun gates and G-GUI on that set**, since none of it was measured.
- **(c)** Waive the rule for named items (take 2.12.3 or zip 9 at 0 days). Each waiver is recorded with its reason; zip 9 would still need calamine (§3.1).
- **Recommendation: (a)**, unless the owner wants the wave's PR open before 10-17, then (b). Under (c) the plan records each waiver in the commit body and E3's matrix.

**DU-25 — rmcp: which version, in how many commits, and the protocol offer.** (supersedes DU-5's (A) and (d))
- Facts [M-today; D]: one step 0.16.0 → 3.5.0 compiles and passes the whole suite with the archived source hunks plus the fifth annotation helper; 3.5.0 is eligible (09-28), 3.5.1 (10-05) is not until 10-12; 3.5.0 moved `ProtocolVersion::LATEST` to `2026-07-28` and our client has no `get_info` override, so its `initialize` offers `2026-07-28` — **the measured re-cut state has no pin**; 3.5.1 adds server-side handling for a client that skips `initialize`, an ungated `object!` macro and auth fixes — `service/client.rs` and both streamable-HTTP client files are byte-identical to 3.5.0's, so it is behaviour-neutral for our features [D: `diff -rq rmcp-3.5.0/src rmcp-3.5.1/src`, rev 3.9; rev 3–3.8 said "client-side"]; `TokioChildProcess`, the streamable-HTTP client and `ContentBlock` are unchanged 3.4.1 → 3.5.1; the archived stage patches target 0.17 / 1.8 / 2.2 / 3.4.1 and none contains the fifth helper; September's `rmcp-stage4-3.4.diff:512` wrote a caret `3.4.1`, which at branch cut resolves 3.5.x — a build nobody compiled.
- **Version:** (v1) **3.5.0** — measured; (v2) 3.5.1 — 2-day waiver today, eligible from 10-12, unmeasured on our code (its diffs touch no file we use [D]); (v3) `=3.4.1` — September's research target, offers `2025-11-25` without an override, needs the archived stage-4 patch re-cut for the fifth helper, and takes a `3.4.1 → 3.5.x` follow-up later.
- **Shape:** (s1) **one commit** 0.16.0 → the chosen version, as measured, carrying P20's constructors, P21's `ContentBlock` and the `docs/api` regeneration, P22's `Option<server_info>`, process-wrap 10.0.1, the five annotation helpers and the gates (real-server round trip, descendant `ps` check); (s2) rev 2.1's four commits (P19 0.17, P20 1.8, P21 2.2, P22 3.x) — bisectable, but each intermediate state must be re-cut against today's `builtins/mod.rs` and the last against the chosen version; none of them is measured on this tree.
- **Protocol offer (DU-5 d, now required for 3.5.x):** (p1) override `NotifyingHandler::get_info` to return `ClientConfig { protocol_version: ProtocolVersion::LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE, ..Default::default() }` (offers `2025-11-25`, the version 3.4.1 offered; `client_info` stays rmcp's default unless the owner separately decides to send ours, rev 2.1 §3.3) — a small source change **not in the measured state**, verified by P19's real-server round trip reading the offered version on the wire; (p2) take the default offer `2026-07-28` and let the round trip decide; (p3) `=3.4.1` (v3) and no override.
- **Requirement spelling:** (r1) `rmcp = { version = "3.5.0", … }` — a caret, so the maintenance bot's lockfile-only refresh moves to 3.5.1 (its job) while this wave's lock pins 3.5.0 (`cargo update -p rmcp --precise 3.5.0`, run twice from a 0.16 lock, and `rmcp-macros` likewise); (r2) `=3.5.0` — the bot cannot move it; a later bump is a manifest commit.
- **Recommendation (rev 3.9): (v4) + (s1) + (p4) + (r1′).** **(v4)** the newest 3.5.x ≥ 7 days old at the cut (3.5.1 today, eligible 2026-10-12T18:35Z) — §9's freshness rule, no `--precise` step-back. **(p4)** `serve_with_lifecycle(transport, ClientLifecycleMode::Auto { preferred_versions: vec![ProtocolVersion::LATEST], legacy_version: Some(ProtocolVersion::LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE) })` in `serve_with_conn` (`client.rs:665-666`): a modern server gets `server/discover` (`2026-07-28`), a legacy one `initialize` offering `2025-11-25`; rmcp's streamable-HTTP client turns a pre-session 4xx (other than 401/403) to `server/discover` into a correlated JSON-RPC error, so a legacy HTTP server falls back too [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/transport/streamable_http_client.rs:375-406`, `transport/common/reqwest/streamable_http_client.rs:284-288`, `service/client.rs:799-835`, `:1044-1057`]; the named costs — a server that ignores unknown methods waits out `DEFAULT_AUTO_DISCOVER_TIMEOUT` (10 s, `:657`) per (re)connect, and 401/403, 5xx, a transport failure or a malformed 2xx fail the connect with no fallback; no `get_info` version pin (`client_info` unchanged, §3.3). **(r1′)** a caret whose floor is the version taken (`rmcp = { version = "3.5.1", … }`). And the stdio child wrapped as a process-group leader, unconditionally (P19). *Rev 3–3.8 recommended (v1) + (s1) + (p1) + (r1) — a patch below the newest eligible and the old lifecycle pinned one revision back: compromises under the owner's rule of 2026-10-12.* Under (s2) P20–P22 come back as separate commits and the count rises by three.

**DU-26 — The Tauri pairing: hold at P04, move the pair in one commit.**
- Facts [M-today: §2.10, §2.3]: the break is real today; the Tauri CLI cannot see the JS packages under Bun; CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri`; the lock holds 2.11.5 (GHSA-w28w-mhc8-qvjv affected); 2.11.6 is eligible and the GHSA fix; the 2.12.1 pair is the newest eligible pair today, the 2.12.3 pair from 10-17 and the one measured (check/clippy/fmt 0 warnings; four bun gates); 2.12 drops Windows 7 (windows 0.62) and the plan records no supported-OS floor; **the hold is measured (rev 3.1)**: `tauri 2.11.6`'s requirements on its siblings are carets, so `--precise` on the four leaf crates alone leaves `tauri-runtime(-wry) 2.12.1` and `tauri-utils 2.10.1` in the lock and `tauri 2.11.6` does not compile against them (19 errors); the ten-crate hold — HEAD's Tauri sub-graph with only `tauri` moved — resolves and compiles the shell clean (`cargo check`, 0 warnings), and leaves the host graph of everything else untouched.
- **(a)** P04 holds the Rust side at **HEAD's Tauri sub-graph with only `tauri` moved to 2.11.6** — ten `--precise` steps in the measured order: tauri-plugin-dialog 2.7.3, tauri-plugin-fs 2.5.2, tauri 2.11.6, tauri-build 2.6.3, tauri-runtime-wry 2.11.4, tauri-runtime 2.11.3, tauri-macros 2.6.3, tauri-codegen 2.6.3, tauri-plugin 2.6.3, tauri-utils 2.9.3 — which keeps wry 0.55.1 / tao 0.35.3 / webview2-com 0.38.2 and `infer 0.19`/`unic-*` until P31a (rev 3's four-crate form is **not an option**: it is a mixed 2.11/2.12 state that does not compile [M-today]; G-GUI at P04 re-runs the measured check with clippy and fmt), then **P31a** moves both locks together to the 2.12 pair — 2.12.3 / tauri-build 2.7.2 / dialog 2.8.2 ↔ `@tauri-apps/api` 2.12.3 / `plugin-dialog` 2.8.2 / `cli` 2.12.2 under DU-23 (a), the 2.12.1 / 2.7.1 / 2.8.1 ↔ 2.12.1 / 2.8.1 / 2.12.1 pair under DU-23 (b) — and narrows the requirements to `tauri = "2.12"`, `tauri-build = "2.7"`, `tauri-plugin-dialog = "2.8"` so that neither a lockfile-only Dependabot PR nor a plain `cargo update` can move a Tauri minor alone.
- **(b)** Take 2.12.x on the Rust side at P04 and the JS side at P31: the tree is mismatched in between. Not recommended.
- **(c)** Hold 2.11.6 for the whole wave (no P31a): the GHSA fix lands, the JS stays 2.11.1 (same minor), the `unic-*` warnings and the GUI-only `infer 0.19` stay (the ten-crate hold keeps tauri-utils 2.9.3 [M-today]), wry/tao do not move, Windows 7 stays supported.
- Sub-row — **the Windows 7 floor**: the owner either accepts the drop (recorded in P31a's body and the release notes) or takes (c). No supported-OS floor is written anywhere today [C: none found].
- Sub-row — **(p-ii)**, the ten-line CI comparison of `tauri*` in `Cargo.lock` with `@tauri-apps/*` in `bun.lock` (DU-15): the narrowed requirements make it a belt over braces; still a new enforcement point.
- **Recommendation (rev 3.9): (a′) — the pair first.** P31a moves to Phase B, right after P03, and moves both locks together (the Rust side by `cargo update -p tauri -p tauri-build -p tauri-plugin-dialog`, then `--precise` to the newest eligible 2.12.x; the JS side by an incremental `bun install`, the v1 lock rewritten from scratch only at P31); P04 is then a plain refresh with **no** Tauri `--precise` step. (a)'s invariant — the pair moves in one commit; Rust never moves a Tauri minor alone — is kept and tightened: `tauri-plugin-dialog` is pinned `=` to the newest version both registries carry (today `=2.8.2`; crates.io also carries crate-only 2.0.3–2.0.5, so plain equality is not always available — a crate-only patch is a hand-made Rust-only commit that says so), `tauri = "~2.12"` and `tauri-build = "~2.7"` (tilde: the minor held — a caret `"2.12"` admits 2.13, rev 3.10) join it in `[workspace.dependencies]`, P35 ignores every update type for the plugin packages, and (p-ii) is withdrawn. Why: (a)'s ten-crate hold existed only so P04 could stay lock-only while the JS half waited for Phase J — measured to compile, but a sequencing convenience; the gui group's stage D **is** the pair-first commit's Rust half (56 lock changes from HEAD's lock; check 0 / clippy 0 / fmt 0 [M-today: gui D]). The pair-alone JS state (the three `@tauri-apps/*` pins on HEAD's other pins, an incremental v1 lock) was never measured as a stage, so P31a's gate runs the four bun gates itself, and `bun audit` stays at HEAD's 24 until P31. The Windows 7 drop stays accepted and recorded (the sub-row). Gone with the hold: the ten-crate list, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/hold-footprint.txt` as a reference, the held reference locks as G-base references (§5.1 item 6b), P18's and P29's interim notes, P31a's ten-step back-out, Appendix B #29, #39, #40. *Rev 3.1–3.8 recommended (a); the owner took it on 2026-10-11, and the owner's rule of 2026-10-12 is what revises it.* Under (c) P31a is dropped and §3.1 gains "tauri 2.12: the Windows 7 decision".

### 4.4 New decisions for Part II (the flexible apps; §7)

Each of these widens a component's job, adds a boundary or an enforcement point, or costs money, and is therefore a decision. None lands under a dependency-bump commit.

**DU-27 — Extract `openalpaca_app_support` (G1).** [facts: architect §1–§2, measured crate counts; sizes from `fn_ranges.py`]
- The shell uses 17 storage items, none SQLite; a database-free crate of ≈1,910 moved lines (paths ≈450, discovery ≈210, daemon_lifecycle ≈180, the project reader incl. `resolve` ≈820, three `fsx` primitives + `sha256_hex` ≈80, reveal ≈170) and 637 lines of test files moving whole, with storage re-exporting every moved item under its old path so the 184 references in 65 files outside storage do not change; the shell's graph 245 → 238 distinct crates (236 under W2); after it no edit under `crates/openalpaca_storage/` rebuilds the shell. Reveal moves **with the one canonical project reader** (A10's correction of A09).
- **(a)** Two commits — Q05 (paths, discovery, lifecycle) and Q06 (project reader, fsx, reveal; the shell leaves storage) — with W1 (the whole discovery module moves; `rand`/`chacha20` stay in the shell's graph), the 46 writer-using tests staying in storage, and the two `DURING_READ` hook tests rewritten onto a 10-line `#[cfg(test)]` fixture writer and moved. **(b)** The same with W2 (feature-gate the writer; saves two crates; adds a feature to reason about). **(c)** The same but the two hook tests kept unchanged through a `test-hooks` feature on support (the `test-home-guard` pattern). **(d)** Do not extract: the shell keeps linking storage; G-GUI keeps its P06/P08/P12-shaped triggers (P12's is dropped either way).
- Name: `openalpaca_app_support` (alternative `openalpaca_host`).
- **Recommendation: (a).** Under (d) §7.3 is dropped and the shell's compile isolation stays [NE]. Under any of (a)–(c) the `test-home-guard` audit extends to the new crate (its tests carry a copy of `HomeStoreGuard`), Q01's discovery byte pin lands first, and the shell's dependency baseline is recaptured after Q06 (G-GUI, §7.7). **Rev 3.9 (the owner's rule):** (a) **without** re-exports — Q05 and Q06 re-point the 184 references in 65 files outside storage to `openalpaca_app_support::…` in the same commits (the daemon and the CLI gain the dependency; storage imports support internally); no `pub use` keeps the old paths alive beside the new ones, and the shell switches once, in Q06.

**DU-28 — HostBridge and a browser dev mode (G3-a).** [facts: architect §3]
- Four `invoke` sites (`lib/connection.ts:79`, `:106`, `:241`; `lib/reveal.ts:43`), one `getCurrentWindow` (`TrafficLights.tsx:18`), one dynamic plugin-dialog import (`AddExtensionForm.tsx:44`), `canReveal` on `globalThis.isTauri` (`reveal.ts:25`); 23 test files `vi.mock("@tauri-apps/api/core")`. Plain Vite is not a daemon-connected app today because `connection.ts:26` imports `invoke` and `:298` bootstraps through Tauri.
- **(a)** A `HostBridge` interface (`ensureDaemonRunning`, `getConnectionInfo`, `readDaemonLogTail`, `awaitDaemonStopped`, `reveal`, `dialog`, `window`) with three hosts: `TauriHost`; `DevServerHost`, a serve-only Vite middleware reading `$OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE/state/discovery.json` **server-side at request time** (expiry + TCP probe; answers `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` origins only; spawns nothing; never emitted by `vite build`, so no token reaches `dist/`) — **and a widening the owner should see** (rev 3.1): the middleware hands the daemon's token to **any local HTTP client that reaches `127.0.0.1:1420`** while the dev server runs — the `Origin` check binds browsers only, a `curl` sets any `Origin` — so the token's reach grows from "a same-user process that can read the 0600 file" to "any local process of any user"; Vite binds localhost (`vite.config.ts:22-27` sets `port: 1420` and `strictPort`, no `host` [C]), so never a remote one; dev-only and serve-only, never in a bundle; `FixtureHost` (replaces the 23 mock blocks; a `?host=fixture` browser mode for layout work). ≈ +350 / −250 LOC in `src/`, ≈ −230 lines of mocks [NE: estimates]. **(b)** (a) plus a fixture daemon (MSW-style canned `/v1/*`, ≈200+ LOC) for a fully offline browser mode. **(c)** Nothing: `bun run dev` stays a layout-only server.
- **Recommendation: (a) now, (b) later** if layout work wants it. Under (c) P31–P33's tests keep their 23 mocks.

**DU-29 — The daemon compatibility contract (G3-b): a new refusal state.** [facts: architect §4]
- `discovery.json` carries `build.version`/`build.protocol` (literals `1` at `discovery/mod.rs:226`, `:241`) that `ConnectionInfo` drops (`:253-267`); `/v1/health` returns `status`, `version`, `pid`, `instance_id` and no protocol (`router.rs:485-492`); the shell reuses any daemon that accepts a TCP connect within 300 ms (`lib.rs:257-268`), past the token's expiry (`:40-49`, T21); a spawn over a live daemon meets the singleton lock and exits (`discovery/mod.rs:180-210`, `main.rs:118`).
- **(a)** `support::protocol::{PROTOCOL, OLDEST_SUPPORTED}` as the one source for `make_discovery`, a new `/v1/health` field `protocol`, additive `version`/`protocol` on `ConnectionInfo` (Rust + TS; `null` from an older shell/daemon); a pure `decide_reuse` with the five-step rule — spawn (no/unparseable file); spawn (TCP refused, health fails, or `instance_id` mismatch — the listener is not this daemon); reuse (`protocol == PROTOCOL`); reuse with a note (`OLDEST_SUPPORTED <= p < PROTOCOL`); **mismatch** (`p > PROTOCOL` or `p < OLDEST_SUPPORTED`): never kill, never spawn, name the versions and the remedy (`openalpaca daemon stop`, then Start daemon, or update the app); the TS reconnect ladder parks on a mismatch like `stopped_elsewhere`. The health probe: (a-i) a ≈40-line hand-written HTTP GET over `std::net::TcpStream` (no new dependency; the shell has no HTTP client in its graph today [M-today]) or (a-ii) a small client crate. T21 is **not** decided here: today's reuse-past-expiry behaviour is pinned as a test and named.
- **(b)** Only the additive fields and the health field, no mismatch state (information only). **(c)** Nothing.
- **Recommendation: (a) with (a-i).** Under (b) the GUI shows versions but still reuses an incompatible daemon; under (c) the question returns the first time a daemon and an app are built from different commits (Part I's E7 already does that deliberately).

**DU-30 — `prepare-sidecar.ts` and the three release scripts (G2); and when.** [facts: §3.3; architect §5]
- **(a)** Rewrite `prepare-sidecar.ts`: always run `cargo build -p openalpacad --locked [--release] [--target <T>] --message-format=json-render-diagnostics` and stage the `executable` of the `compiler-artifact` message for `openalpacad`'s bin target (correct for `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `[build] target-dir`, `--target`; never tauri-build's copy); remove `shouldRebuild`; an explicit `--reuse <path>` for development only (refused with `--release`); in release mode write `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>.sha256` and a `.provenance.json` (HEAD, lock sha256, profile, target, features, rustc) **before** `tauri build`; a GUI-only `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (`target/gui`) by default. And the three package scripts: call `prepare-sidecar.ts --release` first, copy the staged sidecar into `libexec/` (one artifact, one hash), build the CLI alone, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, `--locked` everywhere; verify the bundled copy against the `.sha256` after `tauri build`. **(b)** Only the script's freshness fix (cargo decides; cargo's artifact path), leaving the joint build. **(c)** Nothing: E5/E7 keep rev 2.1's hand sequence.
- **When:** (w-i) before P04, so every identity check in the wave (G-ident, E5, E7) is mechanical; (w-ii) after the wave.
- *Built (rev 3.5), (a) with (w-i):* `bc0e6963`, `71c6e243`, `c5922704` — with rev 3.5's ruling 1 (no-path `--reuse`, the record for every profile, the variable as the dev switch) and ruling 2 (Linux's bundled copy reported, `libexec/` held).
- **Recommendation: (a) with (w-i)** — Q01–Q03 are script and test work that touch no dependency. P37's `--locked`/`--frozen-lockfile` edits fold into Q03 if Q03 lands first. Under (c) E5 and E7 run the hand sequence of §6 (hash the daemon-only release build before `tauri build`, compare bundled and `libexec/`).

**DU-31 — Native CI: a shell-check matrix per PR, a native-smoke nightly (G8); cost.** [facts: architect §7.2; `ci.yml:27-31`, `:37-58`, `:64-78`]
- **(a)** `shell-check` ×3 (`macos-latest`, `windows-latest`, `ubuntu-latest` + webkit2gtk/appindicator/rsvg dev packages) on every PR: `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked` and `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`, an **empty placeholder** at `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` created only in the fresh checkout and deleted after (tauri-build errors when the file is absent [D: `tauri-build-2.6.3/src/lib.rs:41-45`]); after Q06 also `cargo test -p openalpaca_app_support`. This makes DU-18 (b) permanent and widens it to the shell; P01a/P40 are dropped. Plus `native-smoke` ×3 **nightly and on release tags**: a real daemon build, `prepare-sidecar.ts --release`, `tauri build`, then launch → `/v1/health` → `instance_id` stable across close/reopen → explicit stop + lifecycle wait → `daemon.log` tail after a deliberately failing start → Reveal on a store file and a project file, refusals on a missing project and a symlink escape (WebDriver where it works; macOS may only assert launch/reuse/stop) — gaps labelled per OS. **(b)** `shell-check` per PR only. **(c)** Nothing beyond Part I's P01a/P40.
- Cost [NE, reasoned]: `shell-check` cold 10–20 min, warm 2–5 min per OS; `native-smoke` cold 25–45 min per OS (the daemon's 364-crate release build). macOS minutes bill 10×, Windows 2× on a private repository, free on a public one; this repository's visibility is [NE] — it decides the cadence.
- Sub-row — **the supported OS and webview floors, all three platforms** (rev 3.1; the 10-10 audit's G8 asks to "define supported OS/webview versions" and rev 3 carried only DU-26's Windows 7 sub-row): no floor is written anywhere today for macOS (WKWebView), Windows (WebView2) or Linux (webkit2gtk) [C: none found]. (f-a) Adopt the floors tauri 2.12's own documentation states for its three webviews, quoted into the release notes and the README when the decision is taken — those floors were **not read today** [NE], so this plan does not state them; (f-b) stricter floors of the owner's choosing (the machines the app is meant for); (f-c) no floor, today's state, with the Windows 7 question answered by DU-26 alone. Recommendation (f-a) (rev 3.1–3.8). **Rev 3.9: an owner question.** (f-a) read literally declares macOS 10.13 (the bundle default [D: `tauri-utils-2.10.1/src/config.rs:694-696`; Docs: v2.tauri.app/distribute/macos-application-bundle/]), but Tailwind CSS v4 is "designed for Safari 16.4+" and Vite 8's default target is Safari 16.4 [Docs: tailwindcss.com upgrade guide; vite.dev migration guide], and WKWebView is the system WebKit — the app cannot run on the floor (f-a) declares. **(f-b1)** `minimumSystemVersion` 13.3 — Tauri's webview-versions table gives macOS 13.3 → WebKit 615.1.26.11.22 → Safari 16.4 [Docs: tauri-docs `reference/webview-versions.md`] — with Vite 8's default `build.target` and `tsconfig` at its ES year; **(f-b2)** 11.0 plus a stated "Safari 16.4 or later" requirement in the README and the release notes (macOS 11 and 12 reach 16.4 only through a Safari update, which `LSMinimumSystemVersion` cannot express [Docs: Apple's Safari 16.4 release notes]); a lower build target is not available (Tailwind v4 needs 16.4). **Recommendation (f-b1)**; **Q18** applies the answer. The Windows half stays DU-26's sub-row; Linux's is deferred (DU-33).
- **Recommendation: (a)**, nightly for native-smoke. Under (b) WKWebView/WebView2/webkit2gtk rendering stays DU-14 (w-a)/(w-b); under (c) DU-18 (a) stands.

**DU-32 — G4–G6 measure-first, and the G7 protocol they depend on.** [facts: architect §6–§7.1]
- G4 (streaming render): every delta rebuilds every transcript item (`useChatSession.ts:1098-1125` memoizes on the whole `stream.state`; `MessageBody.tsx:117-124` reparses; rows are not `memo`); G5 (latest page): the repository already has `list_recent_by_session` (`repository/conversation/mod.rs:276-299`), used by the context builder, so G5 is "expose the tail on `GET /v1/chat/history`", not a cursor API; G6 (lazy previews): `ChatView.tsx:57`, `:64` import the file/work slots statically (DiffView chunk 88,997 B [A10]). None is a measured jank diagnosis.

- **Rev 3.9:** G4 is measured on the compiled build — `modernization-plan.md` rev 2.0 takes the React Compiler (M69, DU-W1 (b)) — so its change list drops "`memo` the row components; memoize `parseProse` by text" for "manual memoization only where the compiled build's profile shows work the compiler leaves"; the live-row split and the delta coalescing with mandatory flushes stand.

**DU-33 — Platform priority: macOS first (owner, 2026-10-12).** [rev 3.7]
- The owner: "no need to test for linux or windows, focus on mac first". What the sentence covers is the open question: **(a)** every Linux and Windows verification is deferred, local and CI-only alike — rev 3.6's open local runs, P01a/P40 (the wave's temporary Windows `cargo check`), Q08's Windows and Linux legs and DU-31's native-smoke on those systems — while CI's existing jobs (ubuntu as CI's host; the windows-latest Pester job on every push), P01b/P39 and the release scripts' Linux and Windows halves stay as they are; **(b)** only the local runs are deferred, and P01a/P40 and Q08's three legs stay scheduled; **(c)** the existing windows-latest Pester job is removed too.

Under (b) the wave's Windows compile is watched from P01 on; under (c) the Windows installers lose their only test.

## 5. Part I — the dependency wave: commit plan

### 5.1 Before the first commit: owner actions

1. **Take the decisions**: DU-1, DU-2, DU-13, DU-23, DU-25, DU-26 at least (they fix the base, the toolchain, the Bun pin, the versions, rmcp's shape and the Tauri sequence); DU-24 (opener); DU-4, DU-6, DU-10, DU-21 as rev 2.1 asked; Part II's DU-27…DU-32 can wait, except DU-30 (w-i) if the provenance work is to precede P04.
2. **Local Bun at exactly the pinned version, beside the one the owner has** (DU-13; rev 3.1): `BUN_INSTALL="$HOME/.bun-1.4.2" bash -c 'curl -fsSL https://bun.com/install | bash -s "bun-v1.4.2"'` — the install script honours `BUN_INSTALL` as the install root [Docs: bun.com/docs/installation] — and `~/.bun-1.4.2/bin` first on `PATH` in the wave's shells; `bun upgrade` installs the newest release, not the pin [Docs]. **Keep the 1.3.13 install until P35's first bun PR has succeeded**: DU-13 (a)'s condition falls back to (b), and (b) needs a Bun 1.3.x to write a v1 lock from scratch (rev 3 had the owner install 1.4.2 over `~/.bun` and keep a 1.3.x "under (b)" only — the fallback would then have had no Bun to run on). The research never touched the owner's Bun (1.3.13); today's locks were made with a scratch 1.4.2 under `SP/bun142/`.
3. **The DU-10 count and the E6 no-print checks** of every real config file and skill directory, with the **rebuilt** comparators (§6 E6) — before P04 (toml_edit 0.25.x arrives there), P12, P29 and P30.
4. **Backups before the first start of a bumped build on the real store** — rev 2.1 §5.1 item 4 unchanged: stop the daemon; copy `state/openalpaca.db` with `-wal`/`-shm`, every real `config/*.toml` (both copies), `plugins/.permissions.toml`, `plugins/.config/`. The wave adds **no** migration (the database is at schema 4 before and after); the SQLite files are portable both ways by September's measurement on the old schemas [M-sep] and are re-measured on the current ones by E8 before that first start.
5. **The freshness rule, applied on the day of the cut** (DU-23): every target is ≥ 7 days old, or the newest release that is; §1.6 is the list for 2026-10-10 and goes stale daily. Re-run `cargo update --dry-run --verbose` and `bun outdated` at the cut; a newer *patch* of a researched target goes through its commit's gate; a new *major* is outside this plan's evidence (pin the researched major, or research first). **A cut on or after 2026-10-17 takes the measured set.** **Rev 3.9:** the cut **takes** `bun@1.4.3` (P42, its own commit; eligible 2026-10-17T05:24:54Z) and every newer release DU-23's minute table makes eligible, and is recommended at or after 2026-10-17T20:08Z, when the whole Tauri pair is eligible.
6. **G-base, the wave's first gate, on the exact branch point** — rev 3's replacement for rev 2.1's "apply `combined.diff`": on an export (`git archive <base> | tar -x -C …`) or a detached worktree of the base, apply `SP/patches/recut-source-only.diff` whole (45,876 B, 28 files — the four manifests `Cargo.toml`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `crates/openalpaca_core/Cargo.toml` and `crates/openalpaca_mcp/Cargo.toml` included, in their final state; it carries no lock), then regenerate the lock by the step sequence — `cargo +1.99.0 update`, **P04's ten-crate hold in its measured order** (§5.3 P04), the DU-23 (b) step-backs with their `name@locked` spellings if the cut is before 10-17, `cargo update -p rmcp --precise 3.5.0` twice and `rmcp-macros` likewise — and compare it with **`SP/patches/recut-lock-99-final-held.lock`** (rev 3.1 — the final lock cut **with the hold**: `recut-source-only.diff` applied whole to a fresh export of `7295f91b`, `recut-lock-02-update-held.lock` copied in, the three rmcp steps run with cargo resolving the changed manifests on the way, the first `--precise` landing on 3.5.1 and the second on 3.5.0 exactly as P19 says; it differs from the re-cut's `recut-lock-99-final.lock` by precisely the hold — 49 moved / 8 added / 1 removed, 764 → 783 entries, the Tauri crates, wry, tao, webview2-com, the `windows` 0.61 family, `cfb 0.7.3`, the `toml 0.9.12`/`toml_datetime 0.7.5`/`winnow 0.7.15` trio, `infer 0.19.0` and the five `unic-*` kept, `ndk-context` gone — and the workspace minus the shell checks clean on it: `cargo check --locked --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` 0 errors, the `fetch_update` deprecation and the `proc-macro-error2` note as the only warnings, lock unchanged — a compile check, not the suite [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-07-*`, `held-09-wscheck-final-held.log`]; rev 3 compared with `recut-lock-99-final.lock`, which holds the un-held 2.12.3 set, so equality was impossible for the answers it recommends). **The expectation: the regenerated lock equals that file, or every difference is a release dated after 2026-10-10 and is listed with its date and its crate** (the `cargo update` of 06:00 UTC on 2026-10-11 still reproduced `recut-lock-02-update.lock` byte for byte [M-today, rev 3.1]); under DU-23 (b) the six step-backs are a second, expected list. Editing the manifest lines by hand in order is how the P commits are authored, not how G-base runs. Then run `cargo +1.99.0 check --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui`, the **full** suite, `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` (expect exactly `docs/api/crates/openalpaca_mcp.md`), the four bun gates with the chosen Bun on the chosen JS set, and G-GUI on the held Tauri set (0 warnings expected, `held-05-ggui-check-held.log`). Expect 0 errors and 0 failed (rev 3.11: 0 failed apart from P14's seven pins, which must fail — the measured diff predates P14 and carries none of P15–P18's moves; the driver's `P14_PINS`; rev 3.12: that is the branch point's expectation — on a tip carrying all of P15–P18, 0 failed with no pin, and a tree carrying some of them is refused, `expected_pins`); record the counts (today: 5,081/0/16; clippy 66; fmt 1592/306 before formatting — the suite and the lint counts were measured on the un-held lock, whose host graph minus the shell is identical over normal, build and dev edges [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-08-*`, `held-10-*`]; the suite was not re-run on the held lock, so G-base's run is its first). If the tip moved past `7295f91b`, a conflict means re-cutting against the per-step locks `SP/patches/recut-lock-*.lock` with item 6a's driver — **the archived replay driver cannot do it** (R14) and is history. *Rev 3.10:* the whole `recut-source-only.diff` applies by hand through `05541f1b` only — from `41bd92b9` (P24's `tower` lines beside its root-manifest hunk) that hunk fails; the driver, which edits manifests by anchor, is unaffected.
6a. **The G-base driver — a deliverable before P01** (landed as `507f9e8a`, rev 3.5, with the rest of Phase 0; it runs before P03; **its hold, its step-backs and its held references are gone since 6b's commit `95c2aea1`, rev 3.10** — what follows is 6a as written) (rev 3.1; the three audits' one required delta rev 3 left as prose: 10-08 §3 "a separate, portable replay/probe bundle with … propagated nonzero exits", 10-09 P1, 10-10 P1). A script under `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/` (since rev 3.5 tracked as `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/`, `gbase.sh` with its patches, locks and README) that, from one canonical scratch root `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")`, exports the base (`git archive <base> | tar -x -C "$T/src"`) and runs **the eight-step sequence, not one diff plus one `cargo update`**: for each step the manifest edits of that step as anchored `perl` substitutions (each anchor asserted to match exactly once, the re-cut's `edit()`), that step's source hunks, `cargo +1.99.0 update` where the step refreshes the lock plus the step's `--precise` list (P04's ten-crate hold in the measured order; the DU-23 (b) step-backs with their `name@locked` spellings; `rmcp` twice and `rmcp-macros`), `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui`, and a lock snapshot compared with `recut-lock-0N-*.lock` (`recut-lock-02-update-held.lock` at step 2, `recut-lock-99-final-held.lock` at the end); then the full suite, `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`, the four bun gates with the chosen Bun on the chosen JS set, and G-GUI (the empty placeholder sidecar, `dist/` from `bun run build`). **It exits non-zero at the first failed command** — `set -euo pipefail`, every cargo exit code tested, nothing recorded-and-continued — writes every log under `$T/logs/`, and prints at the end the lock differences it found with the dates of the releases behind them. Its seeds are today's drivers, which produced every per-step lock [M-today]: `SP/tools/recut-steps-1-3.sh`, `recut-step-{6a,6b,7,8a,8b}.sh`, `recut-phase-c.sh` with `recut-common.sh`, and rev 3.1's `held-steps.sh`, `held-ggui.sh`, `held-final.sh`, `held-wscheck.sh` — taken **with their two defects removed**: they hard-code `SP` and a worktree path, and they run under `set -u` without `-e`, their `chk`/`run` helpers recording `exit=`/`rc=` and continuing (the pattern the audits rejected in September's `c.sh`/`replay.sh`). Who runs it: the wave's author, at the branch point, before P03 (rev 3.5: Phase 0's no-version commits, P01 among them, landed first), and again whenever the base moves; its result (exit code, the lock differences and their dates, the counts) is recorded in `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 (item 7) with the path of its logs. It exists since rev 3.5 (`507f9e8a`), so item 6's hand procedure is no longer the fallback.
6b. **Rev 3.9 — G-base without the hold.** The driver (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh`) is amended in a no-version commit before P03 (`chore(deps): G-base driver — the pair first, no Tauri hold`): step 2 runs `cargo +1.99.0 update` with no Tauri `--precise` steps (P31a's pair step precedes it); the rmcp step takes no `--precise 3.5.0` (`gbase.sh:376-379`); the references are the shipped **un-held** locks — `recut-lock-02-update.lock` at step 2 and `recut-lock-99-final.lock` at the end — with two expected differences listed: `rmcp`/`rmcp-macros` 3.5.0 → 3.5.1 (DU-25 (v4)) and every release dated after 2026-10-10; its JS set adds the three `@tauri-apps/*` pins. That the pair commit followed by a plain `cargo update` resolves to `recut-lock-02-update.lock` is likely (2.12.3 / 2.7.2 / 2.8.2 are the maxima either way) but was never cut in that order [NE] — the amended driver's first run settles it (Appendix B #46). G-base still applies the measured `recut-source-only.diff`; rev 3.9's source changes (P10's helper, P12's keyring-core, P16's zip 9, P19's `Auto` and wrapper) are measured by their own commits' gates. *Landed (rev 3.10):* `95c2aea1` — the pair is a new step `1b` (P31a's Rust half: the manifest edits, the targeted update, `--precise 2.12.3`) compared with a derived shipped reference, `locks/guiD-lock-01b-pair.lock` (gui stage D's lock hunks over `recut-lock-00-head.lock`); step 2 is a plain `cargo update`; steps 3–7 compare with the un-held snapshots and no hold filter; step 6 takes rmcp with no `--precise`, its one expected difference the exact move `rmcp`/`rmcp-macros` 3.5.0 → 3.5.1 (`lockdiff.py`'s `name:OLD->NEW` spec, so a later 3.5.x stays unexpected; its anchored edit keeps the measured diff's floor `3.5.0` — P19 writes the floor equal to the version taken); a DU-26 pair check after steps 1b and 2 and against `package.json` in the final phase; `lockdiff.py` exits 2 on anything but a finished comparison and `compare_lock` refuses output with no summary line; **`--with-stepbacks` is refused** (DU-23 (b) has no measured pair reference, so the driver cannot replay a cut before 2026-10-17; the removed step-back code is at `d7f21b65:tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh`); `hold-footprint.txt` and the two held locks deleted from the tree (in git history at `d7f21b65`). ### 5.2 Conventions for every commit

Rev 2.1 §5.2's conventions stand (conventional prefixes; a `Verified:` line; the attribution lines; docs that cite a version change in the commit that changes it; the lock produced by cargo in sequence; test hygiene with a sandboxed `HOME`/`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE`/`OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR`, `RUSTUP_HOME`/`CARGO_HOME` at their real values, `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never`, output to files, orphaned `sleep 3600` children killed; the seven-item isolation rule for every manual daemon, CLI or app run). Rev 3 adds and corrects:

- **The scratch root is canonical from the moment it is made** (rev 3.1; lessons 2026-10 — rev 2.1 §5.2 item 1, `plan.md:929`, wrote `T=$(mktemp -d)`): `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")`, once, and every expected path is built from `$T` — the store root Settings → Connection and `GET /v1/status` show (E5 step 4), `project.json`'s `root`, the rows' `project_root` and `storage_path`, E8's snapshot roots. On macOS `/tmp` and `/var/folders/…` both lead into `/private/`, and the daemon records canonical paths, so a literal comparison against `mktemp`'s spelling fails on every correct answer.
- **Onboarding first on a fresh store** (rev 3.1; lessons 2026-10): a fresh store seeds `BOOTSTRAP.md`, and while it exists every turn is onboarding (`Orchestrator::is_bootstrapping`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/orchestrator/mod.rs:698` [C]) — `start_workflow` and the per-request tools are withheld. E4, E5 and §7.5 therefore begin with one attended turn asking for `update_persona` on both documents and a check that `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone, before their first check; a refusal made during onboarding is not an attempt.
- **A sandboxed `HOME` does not keep a test off the machine's secrets** (rev 3.12): a fallback that fires when a file is missing fires *because* `HOME` is empty. A test reaches credential discovery only through `DiscoveryHost` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/credential_discovery/mod.rs`, `9ec986ec`), whose `system()` panics in a unit-test build; a new code path that shells out to `security`, the keyring or a credential file takes its host the same way — no log records a `security` call, so the guard is the check.
- **Every live daemon runs sandboxed from its first launch**: `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` (`tasks/lessons.md`), with `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` and `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` under a temp root and credential discovery off in the seeded `llm.toml`. **The mechanism, by launcher** (rev 3.1 — rev 3 named `OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` alone, a variable only the CLI reads [M-today: `grep` over the tree; C: `apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:10`]): (i) a daemon started by hand — `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <binary>`, its stderr to a file (it writes no `daemon.log`); (ii) a daemon the CLI starts (`openalpaca daemon start`; E7 step 3) — `OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` names a **wrapper script** that does `exec sandbox-exec -f <profile> <real openalpacad> "$@"` (the CLI takes the variable's path as the binary after an is-executable check and no basename check, `manager.rs:241-248`, `:384-392` [C]; `pid_is_daemon` still matches, the exec'd process being `openalpacad`); (iii) the app — the Tauri shell spawns its own staged sidecar and sets only `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` and `MANAGED_LOG_ENV` on it (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:349-350` [C]), so the **app itself** is launched under the profile — `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <app>/Contents/MacOS/openalpaca_gui` — and the sidecar it spawns inherits it (the profile's header names the app's webview reaching its daemon as one of its two uses [C: `loopback-only.sb:1-3`]; that a `setsid`-detached child keeps the sandbox is [Docs: `sandbox-exec(1)`, the profile binds the process and its descendants] and is **proved, never assumed**, by the check below). The app's executable is started directly, never through `open`/Finder (no environment). **The proof, before the first model turn** (lessons 2026-10): from a shell under the same `sandbox-exec` invocation, a request to `127.0.0.1:<port>/v1/health` succeeds and a request to a public host fails; for launcher (iii) also from a `setsid`-detached child of that shell (`python3 -c 'import os,subprocess; os.setsid(); subprocess.run([...])'`), which is the inheritance the sidecar relies on; and the first chat turn, if one is made, shows a `web_fetch` to a public host refused in the daemon's log. Whether WebKit's XPC helpers inherit the profile is [NE] (Appendix B #41) and does not matter to the daemon's confinement.
- **Never start a daemon against a copy of the owner's store** (E8). The current reason is Stage 2: `file_assets.in_project = 1` rows point at **real project files** (`artifacts/mod.rs:464`); a read refreshes their hash/size from the real bytes (`refresh_project_file`, `:1494-1522`); content resolves under the real `project_root` (`resolve_version_path`, `:2050`, `:2084-2116`); `tools::working_folder` returns the project's own root (`working_folder.rs:157-178`, `:377`) [C]. A copied home store neither copies nor isolates those targets; `HOME`/`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` redirect no project root.
- **Synthetic registered projects for every runtime check** that touches files, Open, Reveal, `send`, a missing or re-pointed project, or a delete: a scratch git repository registered through the test daemon's own API (a chat turn or `POST /v1/workspaces`), never a real project. The CLI runs from a scratch directory outside any repository, or with `--global` — the rule stands, with its reason corrected (R31): the CLI resolves its directory through `store::projects::resolve` (`memory.rs:140-143`), nothing is written inside a project's own folder (ST-1), and since Stage 2 the file tools and the shell write the **real** project folder, which makes the rule matter more.
- **One canonical daemon build, hashed before Tauri copies it.** For any packaged check: build `openalpacad` alone (`cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad`), record its `shasum -a 256` **before** `tauri build` (tauri-build copies the staged sidecar into `<target>/<profile>/openalpacad`), and compare the bundled copy and `libexec/openalpacad` to that hash (G-ident). Q02/Q03 make this mechanical; until then it is the E5 sequence of §6.
- **Cleanup waits for the detached daemon**: the shell detaches it (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:362` `libc::setsid()`; Windows `DETACHED_PROCESS` `:372-378`; "the daemon still outlives the window" `:396`) [C], so stop the recorded temporary daemon, wait for process exit **and** lock release (`daemon_lifecycle`), then delete `$T` and the throwaway app data.
- **Mind the `managers::mcp` tests' orphans**: list `ps -o pid,ppid,lstart,command -A | grep 'sleep 3600'` after a daemon run and kill only those with ppid 1 started inside the run (today: two per run [M-today]).

**Named gates** (rev 2.1's five, re-anchored; one new):

| Gate | What it is |
|---|---|
| **G-check** | `cargo +1.99.0 check --locked --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` (`+1.93.0` before P03). `--locked` is accepted by 1.99.0 on the unmodified lock [M-today]: keep it for P03's gate, drop it for the steps that regenerate the lock, restore it afterwards. Expect 0 errors; the only warnings through the whole wave are the `fetch_update` deprecation (from P03) and the `proc-macro-error2` future-incompat note [M-today]. |
| **G-CI** | What CI runs, locally and on the PR: `cargo build --locked`, `cargo test --locked` (`--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`), `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`; `bun run check`, `test`, `format:check`, `build`; Pester. Locally also `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`. Pass = 0 failed and a clean `--check`. Anchors: **5,080 / 0 / 16** — on 1.99.0 measured at `7295f91b` [M-today: `a05-test-199.log`], on 1.93.0 the **recorded** gate at `3810d9d8` [C: CLAUDE.md; four docs-only commits before `7295f91b`; not re-run on 1.93.0 today — recut §A says "recorded"]; **5,081 / 0 / 16** on the re-cut; ≈ 5.5 min a run; the `managers::mcp` flake did not occur in either run — if it does, it counts as the known flake only when the module passes on a rerun. |
| **G-GUI** | By hand (CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri`; Part II's Q08 changes that): `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked` and `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`, **with a `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` of its own**, after `bun run build` (the 2.12 codegen needs `dist/`). tauri-build copies the sidecar into `<target dir>/<profile>/openalpacad` and errors when `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` is absent [D; M-today: the 0-byte copy in `SP/target-gui/debug/`]: **where the real sidecar exists (the main checkout) create nothing**; in a fresh export create an empty placeholder and delete it afterwards. Anchors on the 2.12.3 lock: check 0 warnings (13.9 s cold), clippy **0** unique triples including `openalpaca_storage` (the "2 `let_and_return`" anchor is gone), fmt 0 hunks [M-today]; on P04's held lock (`recut-lock-02-update-held.lock`): check 0 errors, 0 warnings, lock unchanged (13 s with the gui group's warm `target-gui`; `dist/` copied from the main checkout, an empty placeholder sidecar; clippy and fmt **not run** on it) [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-05-ggui-check-held.log`]. Needed after **P31a** (rev 3.9: the pair lands first, in Phase B, so the held-lock anchors above are history), **P04**, **P08** (sysinfo reaches the shell), Q11–Q14, Q17 and at the end; **not** after P12 (keyring never reaches the shell [M-today]); P06 is a trigger only until Q06 lands. |
| **G-lint** | Clippy unique `(file, line, lint)` triples from `cargo clippy … --message-format=json` (level `warning`, primary span under `crates/` or `apps/`), and `Diff in` hunks from `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, both from colour-free logs. Anchors: **78 (1.93.0) → 101 (1.99.0, after P03) → 100 (after P11)** (rev 3.3; the earlier 44/67/66 were a filter error, §0); fmt **1585 hunks / 303 files** on the base, and **every commit leaves the hunk count equal to its parent's** (rev 3.9: until `modernization-plan.md`'s M64 formats the tree — 0 from then on, gated by M65's `cargo fmt --all -- --check`) — so P19 formats the lines its rmcp hunks add (`tools/registry/tests.rs` ×3, `tools/registry/capabilities.rs`, `tools/config/annotations.rs`, `openalpaca_mcp/src/lib.rs`) and P30 the one line its pin test adds (`middleware/skill/tests.rs`), the 7 hunks the re-cut left unformatted [M-today: `c02`]. The project's own 78 (CLAUDE.md, at `3810d9d8`) is this method's 1.93.0 figure. |
| **G-ident** | For any packaged or bundled build (E5, E5b) and E7's pre-wave binaries: the daemon inside the bundle is the one built from the commit under test. **The check**: `shasum -a 256` of the bundled `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad*` == the hash recorded **before `tauri build`** of the canonical daemon-only `--release --locked` build == `libexec/openalpacad` (Q02's `.sha256`, or the hand sequence); **and** version markers derived from the chosen lock, not literals: after the wave `rmcp-<the 3.5.x P19 takes>` ≥ 1 (`rmcp-3.5.1` today, rev 3.9; `rmcp-3.4.1` under DU-25 v3), `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, `3.53.2` ≥ 1 (libsqlite3-sys 0.38.2's header [D]); before it `rmcp-0.16.0` ≥ 1 and `3.51.1` ≥ 1 (`LC_ALL=C grep -c -a -F <marker> <binary>`). That the new markers survive into a **release** binary is inferred from the old ones [M-sep]; E5 checks it. |
| **G-base** (new as a named gate) | §5.1 item 6, run by item 6a's driver: today's re-cut re-run at the branch point, compared with **`recut-lock-99-final.lock`** (rev 3.9: the un-held lock — rev 3.1–3.8 compared with `recut-lock-99-final-held.lock`); equal, or every difference listed — `rmcp`/`rmcp-macros` 3.5.1 (DU-25 (v4)) and each release dated after 2026-10-10 (§5.1 item 6b). |
| **G-bundle** (new, rev 3.9) | The one gate that exercises the CSP, the capability, the webview's handlers and the shell's sidecar spawn before E5 — G-GUI is check, clippy and fmt, `tauri dev` applies no CSP under a desktop devUrl [D: `tauri-2.12.3/src/protocol/tauri.rs:171-183`], and Q11's mock runtime runs no app ACL (`mock_context` carries an empty authority [D: `tauri-2.12.3/src/test/mod.rs:143`]). Build: `bun run build`, then `bunx tauri build --debug --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"com.tangyunpei.openalpaca-gui.wavecheck"}'` (custom protocol + devtools) for leg A, and a **release** bundle for leg B (rev 3.10: a debug shell falls back to an `openalpacad` on `PATH` when the staged sidecar is absent, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:295-299`, so the refusal half needs release); a launch leaves WebKit temp directories under `/var/folders/<user>/{T,C}/` named for the throwaway identifier and for the shell's ad-hoc signing hash (`com.apple.WebKit.*+openalpaca_gui-<hash>`, shared with the owner's own builds of the same tree) — accepted, not removed by an agent with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`; an isolated store, onboarding first. **Leg A — the webview** (rev 3.8's launcher: the daemon first under launcher (i), `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <staged daemon>` with §5.2's two-request proof, then the app's executable unsandboxed with the same store variables, the shell reusing the live daemon): the Web Inspector console shows no CSP or Trusted Types violation and no "IPC custom protocol failed"; connect, log tail, stop and start, Reveal and the folder picker answer; an `invoke` of an ungranted command (`plugin:dialog|save`, a `core:menu` command) typed into the console is refused once an app manifest exists; no duplicate window chrome. **Leg B — the spawn** (launcher (iii), the app's executable under `sandbox-exec` started directly with no daemon running — rev 3.8 kept it for exactly this, the webview being unable to reach the daemon there): the shell locates and starts its staged daemon, proved from outside — `/v1/health` on the port `discovery.json` records, `ps -o pid,ppid,pgid,command` showing the detached child — and, with the staged sidecar removed, the refusal sentence. Attached to P31a, Q04, Q11–Q14, Q17, Q18 and `modernization-plan.md`'s shell commits (M63, M71–M79), each naming the leg it needs. |

**Backing out, in general** (rev 2.1): revert in reverse order; to revert a middle commit restore its manifest line and `cargo update -p <crate> --precise <old>`; the formats marked **[rollback M]** are safe to roll back (the old build reads what the new one wrote — measured in September on the format, and today the whole suite passes on the bumped crates).

### 5.3 The commits

Status in each heading: **Required** (lands under every DU answer except one that removes it), **Recommended (DU-x)**, **Conditional** (P01b, P39 — expected to apply, §2.11), **Optional (DU-x)**. Each entry is marked **unchanged** (rev 2.1's text stands at `7295f91b`), **changed: …** or **new**. "Recut step N" names the re-cut step that measured it (`SP/logs/recut-0N-*`, `SP/patches/recut-lock-0N-*.lock`).

#### Phase A: CI and repository (no Rust change)

**P04 — `chore(deps): refresh Cargo.lock within semver (cargo update on 1.99.0), holding only what is younger than 7 days`** · Required · changed: the move count, the step-back list, **no Tauri hold since rev 3.9** (P31a lands first), the gate

At the recommended cut none of rev 3.1's six step-backs is needed: the releases they stepped back from — `uuid 1.28.0`, `serde_json 1.0.152`, `toml 1.1.8`, `toml_edit 0.25.17`, `tokio-util 0.7.20`, `signal-hook 0.4.5` — are all eligible by 2026-10-17 (DU-23); whatever appeared since is read from `cargo update --dry-run --verbose` at the cut against DU-23's minute table. **No Tauri step** (rev 3.9, DU-26 (a′)): P31a moved the pair already, so the refresh meets 2.12.x and moves only its patch, if any — rev 3.1–3.8's ten-crate `--precise` hold is gone. **Regenerate from the current manifests, never from September's hunks** (R26: core's `libc`; the archived lock hunks fail).
- **Measured result at `7295f91b`** [M-today: recut step 2, the **un-held** refresh]: cargo "Locking 139 packages to highest Rust 1.99 compatible versions"; 145 moved / 3 added / 9 removed by `(name, version)` sets; 22 "behind latest" (the held majors); the same command on a fresh export at 06:00 UTC on 2026-10-11 reproduced that lock byte for byte [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-01-*`]. **With the ten-crate hold** [M-today, rev 3.1: `held-04-held-vs-head-lockdiff.txt`]: **103 moved / 2 added (`core_detect 1.0.0`, `multiversion_no_op 1.0.0`) / 1 removed (`tinyvec_macros`), 732 → 737 entries**; against the un-held refresh the hold is 45 moved / 8 added / 1 removed (the Tauri crates, wry 0.55.1, tao 0.35.3, webview2-com 0.38.2, the `windows` 0.61 family, brotli 8, cfb 0.7.3, `infer 0.19.0` and the five `unic-*` kept; `ndk-context` gone). A08's probe measured `descendant_alive=true` without the wrapper [A08]; with it the fixture shows no descendant, or P19 does not land.

Today `rustls`'s `ring` feature is enabled by `openalpaca_connectors` alone [M: `cargo tree -e features -i rustls@0.23.43`, rev 3.9], while reqwest 0.13's `rustls` brings aws-lc-rs — two providers in one binary (§3.3). With both providers' features enabled on rustls, the explicit install is what makes the process default unambiguous.
