## 6. Verification for Part I

**What CI covers** (first time for some of it): the Linux build and tests (the first Linux compile of zbus/keyring 4, sysinfo 0.39's Linux zombie path, the `managers::mcp` direct-child kill with process-wrap 10); clippy and `--locked` (DU-22); the four bun gates under the pinned Bun; Pester on Windows; the temporary Windows `cargo check` (P01a) or, once Q08 lands, the permanent three-OS shell-check; the advisories jobs (P34).

**What CI cannot cover, and how to cover it.**

| Gap | Why CI cannot | How |
|---|---|---|
| macOS at all | CI is ubuntu and Windows only | Everything today was measured on macOS aarch64 (Darwin 27.0.0); re-run G-CI locally on the final commit (E3). |
| The Tauri crate | CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri` — until Q08 (DU-31) | G-GUI by hand after P04, P08, P31a and at the end. |
| A packaged app, which daemon it bundles, CSS in WKWebView | no bundle job | E5 with G-ident. |
| CSS in WebView2 and webkit2gtk; WebView2 under windows 0.62 | no Windows/Linux desktop here | DU-14 (w-a) accepts, (w-b) runs E5b; DU-31's native-smoke covers it nightly with labelled gaps. |
| Any Windows Rust build; the Windows keychain | no Windows Rust job | P01a (temporary) or Q08 (permanent); the keychain stays unverified. |
| The Linux Secret Service read-back | no session bus on the runner | DU-10: a container with gnome-keyring unlocked, if Linux `use_keychain` users exist. |
| The macOS keychain read-back | never the real keychain | DU-10 (a): a disposable macOS user or VM. |
| Real MCP servers; descendant processes on disable; the offered protocol version | network, third-party servers | P19's round trip, `ps` check and forking fixture. |
| The REPL at a terminal | no TTY | P27's checklist. |
| A live Telegram bot | network, a token | P28's gate, if taken. |
| The owner's real config and skill files | `~/.openalpaca` is off limits to the research | E6, with rebuilt tools. |
| The owner's real partitions and database under the new build | same | E8, on a copy, read-only, no daemon. |
| `docs/api` staleness | CI does not run `gen_api_docs.py --check` | G-CI's local step; E3. |

**The wave's end-to-end gates** (after P38, on the branch, before the merge; every manual run under §5.2, every live daemon under `loopback-only.sb` from its first launch).

- **E1 — CI green** on the PR, including the Windows check (P01a's last green run recorded before P40, or Q08's three `shell-check` jobs).
- **E2 — advisories.** cargo-deny `advisories ok` on the wave's lock; cargo-audit **0 vulnerabilities** (today's re-cut lock: 0, with 4 transitive warnings, exit 0 [M-today]) — naming the five cleared (lopdf -0187, quick-xml -0194/-0195, rmcp -0189, rustls -0285) and the two unsound `lru` warnings gone; `bun audit` 0 (exit 0). The advisory DB is fetched fresh at the gate (today's run used `7eebec69` of 2026-10-09 with `--no-fetch`). If RustSec has imported zip2's GHSAs by then, E2 does what **DU-21's sub-row** decided — under (a) P34's dormant `ignore` is activated with its reason, under (b) zip 9.0.1 is already in — and never decides it itself (rev 3.1).
- **E3 — matrix conformance, locally on macOS** (replaces rev 2.1's "`cargo update --dry-run` locks 0" and "`bun outdated` is empty", which the 7-day rule, optional P32 and any held major make unsatisfiable [A09, A10]): G-CI with 0 failures (the known flake only if it passes on rerun), `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` included; G-GUI; G-lint at **66** unique (≤ 43 after P36) and **1585** fmt hunks (equal to the base — every commit formatted its own lines); and **every line of `cargo update --dry-run --verbose` and `bun outdated` is explained by the approved version/hold matrix** — §1's "newest eligible" column as of the cut, §1.6's collisions under DU-23, §3.1's holds (zip 8.6, axum, teloxide, twilight, rustls 0.24, lopdf 0.42), rmcp 3.5.0 (3.5.1 left to the bot), Bun 1.4.2 — with nothing unexplained. Today the dry run would list at least ten eligible-by-rule holds plus the deliberate ones [M-today].
- **E4 — the memory round trip** on an isolated store with outbound networking denied (slice 3's gate procedure), through the CLI and E5's bundle: it exercises SQLite 3.53.2, the sha2-named partitions (owner key and project-UUID keys), the memory index at schema 6, toml config loading and the Tauri shell together. **First** (rev 3.1, §5.2): the daemon under the profile from its first launch with the two-request proof, then one attended `update_persona` turn and a check that `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone — a fresh store answers every turn as onboarding until then, and a refusal made before that is not an attempt. CLI verbs from a scratch directory outside any repository or with `--global`; the project turn on a **synthetic registered project**; confirm no real project is selected in the app before the first write.
- **E5 — a packaged build of the wave tip, built so that it cannot pick up a stale daemon**, with G-ident. **Rev 2.1's step 2 claimed `prepare-sidecar.ts`'s build is "a no-op after step 1" — false**: the joint `-p openalpaca -p openalpacad` build gives the daemon the CLI's reqwest `multipart` feature and the sidecar hook builds the daemon alone (`apps/openalpaca/Cargo.toml:20`; `prepare-sidecar.ts:60-61`; the two feature graphs [A09]), and tauri-build then copies the staged sidecar over `<target>/<profile>/openalpacad` (§3.3). The sequence, **with Q02/Q03 landed**: `bun run prepare:sidecar:release` (cargo's own artifact, the `.sha256` and `.provenance.json` recorded), `bunx tauri build --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"com.tangyunpei.openalpaca-gui.wavecheck"}'` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`, then compare the bundled `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad*` and the staged copy to the recorded `.sha256`. **Without them**, by hand in a worktree or export of the wave tip (never the main checkout's staged binary): 1. `rm -f apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-*`; `cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad` (**alone**, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` unset because the hook reads `./target/release`); `shasum -a 256 target/release/openalpacad` **recorded now**; 2. `cd apps/openalpaca-gui && bun install --frozen-lockfile && bunx tauri build --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"…wavecheck"}'` — the hook finds its source newer than the absent copy, rebuilds the daemon alone (cargo: up to date) and stages it; 3. G-ident: the bundled daemon's hash == the recorded hash; markers from the chosen lock (`rmcp-3.5.0` ≥ 1, `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, `3.53.2` ≥ 1); if the owner's `package-macos.sh` is used instead, its `libexec/openalpacad` must carry the same hash — which it cannot while the joint build stands (Q03). 4. Launch from the isolated shell (§5.2: the seeded `llm.toml` with discovery off; **rev 3.8: launcher (iii) leaves the webview unable to reach its daemon (Q00) — start the daemon first under launcher (i), then the app unsandboxed with the same store variables; the paragraph's original wording follows** — **the app under `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb …/Contents/MacOS/openalpaca_gui`, directly, so the sidecar it spawns inherits the profile** — rev 3 wrote "the daemon under `loopback-only.sb`" with no mechanism that reaches a sidecar); run §5.2's two-request proof (localhost succeeds, a public host fails, from the sandboxed shell and from a `setsid`-detached child of it) **before the first chat turn**; before any write confirm the store root in Settings → Connection and `GET /v1/status` equals `$T/store` with `$T` canonical (§5.2), the `Config base dir:` line in `daemon.log`, and that no project is selected. **4a. Onboarding** (rev 3.1, §5.2): one attended turn asking for `update_persona` on both documents, then confirm `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone — before 5; a refusal during onboarding is not an attempt. 5. **The checks** — rev 2.1's (the first-run card; Models & keys add a key → restart → it loads; Persona ETag / memory If-Match; Stop daemon then Start daemon; Chat, Memory and Settings in WKWebView against a pre-wave bundle built the same way with a second throwaway identifier and G-ident's pre-wave markers) **plus the current contracts, on synthetic registered projects** (R28): project registration, re-point (`Re-point…`) and `Delete data…`; approval re-entry (`approval-card-returns.test.tsx` is the unit twin); the working state card; an upload and an artifact's Open and Reveal; the memory flows; **Stage 2** — a project `file_write`/`file_edit` with its confirmation card and the pre-edit snapshot under `sessions/<id>/snapshots/` (`pre_edit.rs:47` `check_pre_edit`, `:86` `keep_pre_edit_copy`), a projectless scratch write (`scratch/<conversation id>/`), a pointer row beside a by-product at the same address (`Results in the project` filter, no History/Diff), a missing/re-pointed project's `410 ARTIFACT_PROJECT_MISSING` on Preview/Open/Export and Reveal's refusal, and a project delete that keeps the real project file. 6. **Cleanup**: stop the recorded temporary daemon, wait for exit and lock release (the shell detached it, `lib.rs:362`), then `rm -rf "$T"` and the `~/Library` entries named after the throwaway identifier.
- **E5 additions (rev 3.9).** On the packaged bundle, under rev 3.8's launcher (the daemon first under launcher (i), the app unsandboxed with the same store variables): Library **Export** clicked once before `modernization-plan.md` M76 (expected today: nothing saved while the toast says "exported" [NE]) and after it (the file in Downloads, a second export under a distinct name); the Extensions row's **Authorize** link (a fixture MCP server answering 401 with a hint) and a link inside a markdown preview, each opening the default browser with the app staying (M77); `LSMinimumSystemVersion` read from `Info.plist` once Q18 lands; the Web Inspector console free of CSP and Trusted Types violations and of "IPC custom protocol failed" (M71–M73); no duplicate window chrome (M74).
- **E5b** (DU-14 w-b, or DU-31's native-smoke): the same screens in a Windows (WebView2, now `webview2-com 0.39` under windows 0.62) and a Linux (webkit2gtk) build of the wave tip against pre-wave builds; G-ident applies to each. Also P27a's Linux Open.
- **E6 — the owner's real data, read-only, printing only equal or different, with rebuilt tools** (R13). Before any tool touches a real file: build each old/new pair (`tomlcmp` 0.9.12 ↔ 1.1.x; `tedit` 0.25.13 ↔ the version P04's lock takes; `yamlcmp` serde_yaml ↔ serde-saphyr 1.3 with the P30 options) **from one sanitized source differing only in the dependency** — the archived `tedit-0.25.15` is not that (its `:114` `DIFF` diagnostic) and the archived `yamlcmp` **writes** `render-*.yaml` beside its corpus and walks every `.md` — remove the serializer branch, enumerate the real `SKILL.md` inputs explicitly, print only an index and an equality bit (never a value, key or path fragment), and prove on a synthetic trigger path that the inputs and their neighbours are byte-unchanged after a run. Report separately: parse failures, cross-version differences, and the four shared `reparse_equal=false` round-trip defects [A08]. Include **typed** probes built from current source (`load_daemon_config` with `carry_over_turns`, without `episode_max_per_day`/`compaction_model`; `render_config`; the M3 `llm.toml` edit; `McpConfig`; `AgentConfigFile`; the plugin manifest). The files: rev 2.1 §6 E6's list unchanged (`llm.toml`, `daemon.toml`, `mcp.toml` in `~/.openalpaca/config/` and in the checkout's `config/`; any other `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR`; `plugins/.permissions.toml`, `plugins/.config/*.toml`, each `plugins/*/plugin.toml`; `config/tools/*.toml`, legacy `config/agents/*.toml`; the skill directories incl. plugin-contributed). The DU-10 count over every `llm.toml`. Runs before P04, P12, P29 and P30.
- **E7 — a rollback rehearsal against E5's isolated store with a pre-wave build whose identity is checked, from the same application source.** `<pre-wave>` is the **wave's base commit** (a post-Stage-2 commit: `7295f91b` today or later; never `d484a56a`, which registers only schemas 1–3 and refuses a schema-4 file as "newer than this build" [M-today]). 1. In an export of `<pre-wave>` (`git archive … | tar -x -C $T/prewave`), `cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad` **alone** (the daemon-only invocation, the same feature graph as the sidecar's; the CLI built separately) with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=$T/prewave-target`. 2. G-ident pre-wave markers on `$T/prewave-target/release/openalpacad`: `rmcp-0.16.0` ≥ 1, `3.51.1` ≥ 1 [M-sep: the markers exist in a pre-bump release build]. 3. Quit E5's app, stop its daemon (wait for exit + lock), start the pre-wave daemon in the isolated shell (`OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` naming §5.2's **wrapper script** around `$T/prewave-target/release/openalpacad` — `exec sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb $T/prewave-target/release/openalpacad "$@"` — the pre-wave CLI's `openalpaca daemon start`, from a scratch directory; the two-request proof before any turn) and check: the database opens (schema 4 both sides; files portable [M-sep on the old schemas, E8 on this one]); the key added in E5 decrypts (the provider lists its key as loaded) [M-sep]; the config reads; the synthetic project registered in E5 is listed and its pointer row resolves.
- **E8 — a rehearsal on a copy of the owner's real store, read-only, no daemon, before the first start of a bumped build on the real store.** It is the only check of the owner's existing partitions, snapshots and database under SQLite 3.53.2 and sha2 0.11 on schema 4 / cache 6. 1. Stop the real daemon (wait for process exit **and** lock release, `daemon_lifecycle`); `cp -Rp ~/.openalpaca "$T/realcopy"` (database with `-wal`/`-shm`, `state/memory-control/`, `memory/`, `projects/`, `scratch/`, `config/`); **the real daemon stays stopped until step 6 is done** (rev 3 said "restart if wanted" here; withdrawn in rev 3.1 — step 2 says why; step 9 is where it restarts). The copy holds `.master_key` and encrypted keys: keep it under `$T`, delete it afterwards. 2. In the isolated shell: `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE=$T/realcopy`, `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR=$T/realcopy/config`, `HOME=$T/home`, a scratch working directory outside any repository. **Then delete `$T/realcopy/state/discovery.json` and `$T/realcopy/state/openalpacad.lock` from the copy, or assert both are absent** (rev 3.1): `openalpaca memory doctor` branches on `crate::manager::is_daemon_running()` → `daemon_lifecycle::running_daemon_pid()` → `read_discovery()` → `pid_is_daemon(pid)` [C: `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory.rs:693`, `apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:120-122`, `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/daemon_lifecycle.rs:122-125`], and under `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE=$T/realcopy` that reads the **copy's** `discovery.json`, which `cp -Rp` carried over; a graceful stop removes the file (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:908`) but a forced exit does not (CLAUDE.md), so a copy carrying a pid that is alive again would make steps 5–6 call `Api::connect(&at)?.get("/v1/memories/doctor")` on the **real** daemon with its real token and compare the real store with itself. **Never start a daemon on the copy**: the learner's boot catch-up would call the model with the copy's real keys and write into each turn's real project root, and — Stage 2 — the copy's `in_project = 1` rows point at **real project files** which no copy isolated (§5.2). 3. **A raw read-only connection first, before any application opens the copy** (R08): `sqlite3 'file:$T/realcopy/state/openalpaca.db?mode=ro'` (or the rebuilt probe in `mode=ro`) — record `schema_version` (expect 4), every `sqlite_master` entry (tables, index definitions — `idx_file_assets_addr` on `(COALESCE(project_root, ''), in_project, rel_path)`), per-table row counts, the count of `file_assets` rows with `in_project = 1` and their distinct `project_root` values (**inventory only — never stat or read those paths**), and the retryable-`Error` extraction rows P15 will re-extract. Both CLIs' `memory doctor` open the **migrating** database (`memory.rs:697` → `:1737`), so this capture is the baseline, not the doctor's. 4. **An independent inventory** (R10): `projects/*/project.json` (id, root, version), every owner directory under `memory/owners/` and `projects/*/memory/owners/`, every `state/memory-control/*.json` (partition key, bound root or project id, note count, tombstones) — the expected partition set, against which both doctors' reports are checked; a doctor that omits a snapshot whose id no folder records (`doctor.rs:168-172`; pinned by `memory_files/tests.rs:4194`) or continues after a listing error (`:154-160`) is **explained** by this inventory or the rehearsal fails. 5. **The pre-wave CLI** (E7's binaries): `openalpaca memory doctor --global`, no `--repair`; save the report. **Pass condition for steps 5 and 6** (rev 3.1): the saved output's first line is `No daemon is running; scanning locally.` (`memory.rs:696`) — a report without it came over HTTP from a daemon, and the rehearsal is void. **Home partitions will read as quarantined in both builds**: a home snapshot binds its absolute root (`snapshot.rs:283`) and the copy is at `$T/realcopy` — equal reports are relocation, not healthy reads (R09). Project partitions bind by id and live under the copied `projects/<name>/memory/`, so they are readable in place in the copy. 6. **The bumped CLI**, same command: the same report, partition for partition. 7. **The rebuilt current-source probe** (old/new variants from `<pre-wave>`, differing only in rusqlite/sha2): in read-only mode, `PRAGMA integrity_check`, `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`, FTS5 `integrity-check` on **all three** tables of the cache copy, the same row counts as step 3 (equal), the same search over the index copy by both variants (same ids); and — the part that stands in for the home partitions' healthy read — for every control snapshot, re-derive each note file's revision hash and the partition key with both variants' `sha256_hex` and compare with the snapshot's recorded revisions, printing mismatch **counts** only, writing nothing (no `MemoryIndex::open` that would recreate a cache; no snapshot write; never `doctor --repair` on unknown state). 8. Reopen the copy with the pre-wave variant: equal integrity results and counts. 9. `rm -rf "$T"`; only now restart the real daemon, if wanted. The archived `sqlite-probe` is **not** this probe (R12: no `src/database/`, registries 43/44/45, cache 3, two FTS tables, failures printed not returned).

**Load and timing.** A full suite is ≈ 5.5 min here [M-today]; measure on a quiet machine or rerun a flaky module before calling a failure a regression.

**Runner images.** The PR runs in the 2026-10-19 … 2026-11-19 window: P01b pins `ubuntu-24.04`, P39 reverts it last and its own run is the deliberate move to 26.04.

## 7. Part II — the flexible apps

### 7.1 Goal and assumption

The owner asked for apps that are "more flexible". Rev 3 reads that as the 10-10 audit's G1–G8 — a thin Tauri 2 shell over the independently running daemon (one installer, React + Vite, a small native shell, the daemon owning migrations, agents, tools and the API; the GUI and the CLI sharing it; closing a window never stops background work), a browser-first React dev loop, and low memory / fast startup / fast compilation **measured before promised**. One sentence from the owner replaces that reading. No framework migration, no Tauri 3 (alpha only), no CEF: the current boundary is improved first (§7.9).

Everything quantitative here is a crate count from `cargo tree --locked` [M-today] or a line count from `fn_ranges.py` [M-today]; **no compile time, binary size, RAM or startup figure exists** — nothing was built or launched for Part II [NE]. The frontend-asset numbers (740,645 B JS, a 1.15 s `vite build` [A10]) are not startup or memory evidence.

### 7.2 The architecture (the architect's blueprint, `SP/reports/architect.md`)

**G1 — the shell leaves `openalpaca_storage`.** The shell (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`; `main.rs` uses nothing) uses **17** storage items, all file-system or process-table reads [C]: `discovery::read_discovery` (`:27`, `:45`, `:58`), `ensure_not_expired` (`:31`), `ConnectionInfo` (`:26-61`), `Discovery` (`:257`), `daemon_lifecycle::wait_for_daemon_exit` + `STOP_TIMEOUT` (`:170`), `StopOutcome` (`:124-149`), `store::daemon_log_path` (`:71`, `:103`, `:325`), `read_log_tail` (`:72`, `:104`), `logs_dir` (`:324`), `rotate_daemon_log` (`:326`), `MANAGED_LOG_ENV` (`:350`), `ensure_store(&StoreScope::Home)` + `StoreScope` (`:312`), `ensure_runtime_config_dir` (`:313`), `revealable_path` + `RevealRefusal` (`:198`). Its manifest also declares `directories` and `serde_json`, which `lib.rs` never names [M-today]. What those pull: `daemon_lifecycle` → `sysinfo` (`:76-78`), `read_discovery`, the singleton lock (`file-lock`); `reveal` → `home_root` and **`projects::list`** (`reveal.rs:92`, `:115`) → `list_in` → `read_project_dir` → `fsx::read_if_exists`/`read_regular_nofollow`, `serde_json`, `canonicalize`, `root_is_missing`, the duplicate-id filter, `missing_project_sentence` — and through `lookup`, `folder_name`/`hashed_folder_name` (`unicode-normalization`, `sha256_hex` → `sha2`), `canonical_root` (`fsx::canonical_path_lenient`), `ProjectId` (`uuid`); `ensure_store(Home)` → `directories::BaseDirs`, the README const, `ensure_layout` (`uuid::new_v4`, `write_atomic`); the discovery reader → `serde`, `serde_json`, `chrono` [C]. **So Reveal needs the one canonical file-backed project reader, not only `home_root`** (A10's correction of A09) — and that reader needs no SQLite.

The graph [M-today: `cargo +1.99.0 tree -p openalpaca_gui -e normal --locked`, dedupe and `--no-dedupe`]: **245** distinct `(name, version)` crates; **60** under storage; **226** reachable without it; **18** reachable only through storage. After the extraction **7 leave** — rusqlite 0.38.0, libsqlite3-sys 0.36.0, hashlink 0.11.1, hashbrown 0.16.1, fallible-iterator 0.3.0, fallible-streaming-iterator 0.1.9, similar 3.2.0 — and 9 under W2 (+ rand 0.10.2, chacha20 0.10.2, the discovery writer's); sysinfo, objc2-io-kit, unicode-normalization, tinyvec, chrono, iana-time-zone, num-traits and file-lock **stay** through support; sha2 (also tauri-codegen), uuid (also tauri-utils, tauri-codegen, schemars, cfb), base64 (also plist, swift-rs, tauri-codegen), serde_json, tracing, anyhow, libc stay whatever happens. **245 → 238 (236)** — a small drop; the gain is that **no edit under `crates/openalpaca_storage/` rebuilds the shell**, which is G1's purpose. The shell has no HTTP client in its graph (reqwest/hyper/ureq: 0) [M-today]. For comparison: the daemon's normal graph 364, storage's own 58 [M-today].

**The `openalpaca_app_support` crate** (name a proposal; `openalpaca_host` the alternative). Direction: shell → support; storage → support (storage re-exports every moved item under its old path, so the **184 references in 65 files** outside storage do not change [M-today: grep]); the daemon and the CLI keep depending on storage. Support never depends on storage, core or the daemon. Manifest: storage's (`crates/openalpaca_storage/Cargo.toml:9-24`) minus `rusqlite`, `similar` (and under W2 `rand`): `anyhow serde serde_json chrono directories file-lock sysinfo uuid unicode-normalization sha2 tracing` + `libc` (unix). Two commits (sizes are `fn_ranges.py`'s `loc+doc`, four ranges spot-checked [M-today]):

| Commit | Moves | ≈ lines | Stays in storage |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** (Q05) | `support::paths` from `store/mod.rs` (the env/const names, `home_root`, `resolve_home_root` → `pub`, `state_dir`, `database_path`, `discovery_path`, `lock_path`, `master_key_dir`, `logs_dir`, `daemon_log_path`, `MANAGED_LOG_ENV`, the log rotation and tail (`rotate_daemon_log`, `rotate_log`, `log_generation`, `read_log_tail`, `log_tail`, `strip_ansi`), `cache_dir`, `backups_dir`, `plugins_dir`, `runtime_config_dir`, `ensure_runtime_config_dir`, the Home arm of `ensure_store` as `ensure_home_store()`, `layout_version`, `install_id`, `layout_text`, `create_private_dir`, `write_new`, `write_atomic`, `ensure_layout`, `read_install_id`/`read_layout_value`, `HOME_README`); `support::discovery` (the whole module under W1: `Discovery`/`Listen`/`Auth`/`Build`, `read_discovery`, `ensure_not_expired`, `is_process_alive` ×2, `acquire_single_instance_lock`, `ConnectionInfo` + `From`; and the writer `generate_token`, `write_discovery_atomic`, `remove_discovery`, `make_discovery`); `support::daemon_lifecycle` whole | ≈450 + ≈210 + ≈180 = **≈840**, + tests: `discovery/tests.rs` (3 tests, 30 lines), the lifecycle tests (9, 200 lines), ≈30 of `store/tests.rs`'s 42 by subject | `open_home_database` (opens SQLite), `StoreScope`, `ContentKind`, `store_root`, `content_dir`, `create_below`, `project_folder_gone`, `project_root_of`, `relative_to`, `sessions_dir` (dispatch to `projects::lookup` or create content dirs); `ensure_store`'s project arm becomes a two-line wrapper |
| **B** (Q06) | `support::projects` (reader): the consts, `ProjectId` + impls, `folder_name`/`hashed_folder_name`/`name_parts`/`path_hash`, `canonical_root`/`overlaps_home_store*`, `ProjectFile`/`Project`, `ProjectError` + `is_not_registered`, `projects_dir*`, `lookup`/`lookup_in`/`recorded_in`/`as_named`/`recorded_exactly_in`, `read_project_dir` (→ `pub` `#[doc(hidden)]`; called by `project_life.rs:350`, `project_life/delete.rs:689`), `vanished`, `missing_project_sentence`, `root_is_missing`, the `cfg(test)` read hooks, `unreadable`/`WARNED`/`first_warning`, `list`/`list_in`/`by_id`/`by_recorded_root`/`unfinished_repoint_at`, `ResolvedProject`/`GIT_MARKER`/`resolve`/`resolve_as_written`/`resolve_missing`; `support::fsx` (`read_regular_nofollow`, `read_if_exists`, `canonical_path_lenient` + `sha256_hex`); `support::reveal` whole | ≈820 + ≈80 + ≈170 = **≈1,070**, + tests: `store/reveal/tests.rs` (21 tests, 407 lines), the reader-only share of `store/projects/tests.rs` (44 of 65) and `resolve_tests.rs` (7 of 28) | the test guard (`test_home_guard_armed`, `unguarded_home`, `register_home`, `refuse_unguarded_test_home`, `AFTER_HOME_RESOLVED`), `PROJECTS_LOCK`/`lock`, `register`, `name_for*`, `write_project_file`, `build_project_folder`, `resolve_and_register`, `choose` (≈290); the 48 writer-using tests (21 + 21 + 6) stay; `memory_files::fsx` and `sha256_hex` re-exported so the memory layer keeps one implementation |

Visibility and test consequences [C; M-today]: `read_project_dir` becomes `pub`; `resolve_home_root` becomes `pub` (`register_home` calls it, `projects.rs:845`); the `cfg(test)` `DURING_READ` hook is set only by `store/projects/tests.rs:1154` for two tests (`:1163`, `:1192`) that call `register` only to make a folder and then `fs::rename` it mid-read — they move to support onto a 10-line `#[cfg(test)]` fixture writer (DU-27 a) or stay through a `test-hooks` feature (c); `AFTER_HOME_RESOLVED` is set only by `projects/tests.rs:1077` (register's tests, which stay). The `test-home-guard` feature stays on storage with `register`; what the guard needs from the reader (`resolve_home_root`, `HOME_STORE_ENV`, `STORE_DIR_NAME`) moves as `pub`; support's tests carry their own `HomeStoreGuard` copy from day one, and the **guard audit** (every test that resolves the home store holds a guard; CLAUDE.md *Gotchas*) extends to the new crate. A dev-dependency from support back on storage is **not** an option (it defeats the point).

**Acceptance (G1 done):** `cargo tree -p openalpaca_gui -e normal --locked --prefix none | sort -u` contains none of `openalpaca_storage`, `openalpaca_core`, `openalpaca_llm`, `rusqlite`, `libsqlite3-sys`, `hashlink`, `similar`; `cargo tree -p openalpaca_app_support -e normal --locked` contains no workspace crate; `cargo test -p openalpaca_app_support` green with the moved tests; `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage` green with the 48 writer tests; the workspace suite and the four bun gates unchanged; the measured crate count (expect 238, or 236) recorded in Q06's body — never the audit's "31 leave" figure (a dedupe artefact [M-today]).

**Risk — the discovery wire format.** The daemon writes `discovery.json` with `serde_json::to_vec_pretty` (`discovery/mod.rs:95`; field order = struct order; chrono's RFC 3339 with a variable fraction) and the CLI and the shell read it; **no test pins the bytes** [M-today]. Moving the types changes no derive and should be byte-identical — "should" is the gap. **Q01** lands first: `discovery_json_bytes_are_pinned` (a `Discovery` with fixed `DateTime` literals and a fixed token, `to_vec_pretty` equal to a literal), the `ConnectionInfo` serde names exactly `baseUrl`/`token`/`instanceId`, a captured real file round-tripped; kept in storage as a cross-crate check and copied to support.

**G3-a — HostBridge** (DU-28). Every `@tauri-apps/*` reach under `apps/openalpaca-gui/src` [M-today: grep, non-test]: `lib/connection.ts:26` (`invoke` import), `:79` (`get_connection_info` / `ensure_daemon_running`, validated by `isConnectionInfo` `:59-67`), `:106` (`read_daemon_log_tail`), `:241` (`await_daemon_stopped` → `DaemonStopReport` `:220-231`); `lib/reveal.ts:13`, `:43` (`reveal_path`), `:25` (`canReveal` on `globalThis.isTauri`); `components/shell/TrafficLights.tsx:18`, `:24-27`, `:40-42` (`getCurrentWindow` → `isDecorated`/`close`/`minimize`/`toggleMaximize`; browser fallback `settle(false)`); `views/settings/AddExtensionForm.tsx:44-45` (dynamic `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` `open({ directory: true })`). Consumers: `lib/events.ts:187-188`, `lib/http.ts:159`, `lib/chat-stream.ts:526`, `lib/api/telemetry.ts:73`, `hooks/useConnection.ts`, `lib/daemon-control.ts:50`, `views/settings/ConnectionSection.tsx:60`, `:332`, `:372`, `hooks/useFiles.ts:78`, `canReveal()` at `components/chat/FilePanel.tsx:331`, `components/chat/WrittenArtifactCard.tsx:102`, `views/library/LibraryDetailHeader.tsx:174`, `<TrafficLights/>` at `components/shell/NavRail.tsx:59`; **23 test files** `vi.mock("@tauri-apps/api/core")`. The interface (`lib/host/types.ts`): `HostBridge { kind: "tauri" | "dev-server" | "fixture"; ensureDaemonRunning(); getConnectionInfo(); readDaemonLogTail(lines); awaitDaemonStopped(); reveal: null | { label(); inFolder(path) }; dialog: null | { pickDirectory() }; window: null | { isDecorated(); close(); minimize(); toggleMaximize() } }`, with `ConnectionInfo` gaining `version: string | null; protocol: number | null` (§ G3-b). `lib/host/index.ts` chooses once at module load: `isTauri()` from `@tauri-apps/api/core` → `TauriHost` (rev 3.9: the official check, not the hand-read global; `TauriHost` tested through `mockIPC`, Q07) (≈120 LOC: the four `invoke`s with today's validation, the window and dialog wrappers); `import.meta.env.DEV && VITE_OPENALPACA_HOST === "dev-server"` → `DevServerHost` (≈40 LOC + a ≈80-LOC Vite plugin, `apply: "serve"`, `configureServer`: `/__openalpaca/connection` and `/__openalpaca/log-tail?lines=` read `$OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE/state/discovery.json` server-side at request time with the shell's rules — expiry, a TCP probe — answer only `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` origins, spawn nothing, never run under `vite build`); `… === "fixture"` → `FixtureHost` (≈60 LOC; `{ connection, logTail, stopReport }`; `vitest.setup.ts` and `?host=fixture`); otherwise `NoHost`, every method rejecting with today's `ConnectionError`. `setHost(h)` for tests. `connection.ts` (369 lines) keeps the cache, listeners, stop intent and URL helpers; `reveal.ts` becomes a 15-line facade. Net ≈ +350 / −250 LOC in `src/`, ≈ −230 lines of mocks [NE: estimates]. **Why no token in the bundle**: the daemon mints a 32-byte token at every boot (`discovery/mod.rs:236-240`), writes it 0600 (`:120-124`), expires it after 24 h (`:239`), and every `/v1/*` call carries it (`http.ts:146-148`; SSE/WS `?token=`, `connection.ts:355`, `:363`); CORS is already `permissive()` (`router.rs:480`), so the only gap is how a page gets the current token — a dev-server-side read of the 0600 file at request time, never a build-time constant [C].

**G3-b — the compatibility contract** (DU-29). Today [C]: `discovery.json` carries `schema: 1`, `instance_id`, `pid`, `started_at`, `listen {host, port}`, `auth {token, issued_at, expires_at}`, `build {version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION") = "0.1.0", protocol: 1}` — the `1`s are literals at `discovery/mod.rs:226`, `:241`; `ConnectionInfo` (`:253-267`) keeps `base_url`, `token`, `instance_id` and drops the rest (TS mirrors it, `connection.ts:28-32`); `GET /v1/health` (`router.rs:16`, `:485-492`) returns `status`, `version`, `pid`, `instance_id`; `useHealth` polls it every 30 s and clears the client on an `instance_id` change (`hooks/useConnection.ts:64-73`, `:199`); the shell reuses any discovery whose `listen` accepts a TCP connect within 300 ms (`lib.rs:257-268`), even past the token's expiry (`:40-49`; T21) while `get_connection_info` refuses an expired one (`:31`); a spawn over a live daemon meets the singleton lock (`discovery/mod.rs:180-210`; `main.rs:118`) and exits. Proposed: `support::protocol::{PROTOCOL, OLDEST_SUPPORTED}` read by `make_discovery`, `/v1/health` (new `protocol` field) and the shell; `version`/`protocol` additive on `ConnectionInfo` (Rust + TS); liveness in two steps (TCP, then `GET /v1/health` with a 300 ms budget parsed for `instance_id` and `protocol`); `decide_reuse`, a pure function with the world injected (like `daemon_lifecycle::wait_for_exit_with`), with the five-step rule of DU-29 (a) and a `Mismatch { daemon_version, daemon_protocol, shell_protocol, remedy }` rejection the TS side reads as `ConnectionError.kind = "mismatch"`, parked like `stopped_elsewhere` (`connection.ts:143`), **never** retry-spawned by `events.ts`'s ladder. Tests: table-driven over `decide_reuse` (compatible; older-supported; newer; missing `protocol` in health and in discovery; malformed discovery; stale pid with a dead port; a wrong server at the old address — health returns 404 HTML; an instance change between discovery and health; an expired token with a live daemon — **T21's current answer, pinned**); the `/v1/health` JSON pinned in `apps/openalpacad`; TS: `connection.test.ts` (the `mismatch` kind, the optional fields), `events.test.ts` (no reconnect-spawn on mismatch), `useConnection.test.tsx` (the note); native: a shell against a daemon built with `PROTOCOL + 1` (a test-only env override) shows the mismatch state and leaves the daemon running. `docs/api` regenerated.

**G2 — build provenance** (DU-30): §3.3's two defects and the fix of DU-30 (a): cargo decides freshness and names the artifact (`--message-format=json-render-diagnostics`, the `compiler-artifact` message for `openalpacad`'s bin target — right for `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `[build] target-dir`, `--target`; never tauri-build's copy, because cargo writes the artifact before any GUI build script runs); `--reuse <path>` for dev only; `.sha256` + `.provenance.json` before `tauri build`; `target/gui` by default; one canonical daemon-only build staged into both `libexec/` and the sidecar; `--frozen-lockfile` and `--locked` in the three scripts; the triple from `--target` when given, else `rustc -vV`, asserted equal to `tauri build`'s. Whether Bun's `execSync` buffers a 364-crate build's JSON without limits is untested — use `spawnSync` with a large `maxBuffer` or stream lines [NE]. **Gate** (in a temporary worktree, never over the main checkout's staged binary): a one-line source edit in `apps/openalpacad/src/args.rs` changes the staged hash; `touch` of the staged file does not skip cargo and the staged hash equals cargo's artifact; `--release` and debug stage different hashes; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/x` stages `/tmp/x/<profile>/openalpacad`; `--target <host>` stages `…/<triple>/<profile>/openalpacad`; after `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` in the default target dir the staged file is unchanged and the next prepare still resolves cargo's artifact; bundled == staged `.sha256` == `libexec/`.

**The four dev loops** (G2's other half, from the 10-10 audit): React/CSS — Vite HMR with the shell and daemon running, no Rust compile (already true; `vite.config.ts:26-27` excludes `src-tauri` from the watch); browser UI study — the fixture host, or the dev-server host against an isolated development daemon; daemon logic — build and restart the development daemon alone, the GUI's `instance_id` invalidation doing the rest; native integration — build shell + support with `--reuse` of a compatible development sidecar; release — frozen JS install, locked Rust build with explicit target/profile, the verified daemon, the production assets, the bundle. `tauri.conf.json:7-9`'s `beforeBuildCommand`/`beforeDevCommand` couple the sidecar build to dev and build today [C]; `--reuse` is the dev-side decoupling.

### 7.3 The commit sequence (Q)

**Q00 — B0 baselines** (a measurement, not a commit): on the frozen application commit, before P01: the §7.5 protocol's scenarios on a release package built by E5's hand sequence and launched as §5.2 says (the app under `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb`, the two-request proof, onboarding first); the shell's dependency graph (`cargo tree -p openalpaca_gui …`, today 245); the Rust and frontend baselines of G-base; the build-time scenarios. Recorded under `tasks/deps-upgrade/baselines/<date>/` (local, like this plan) and cited by every later Q commit's body. *Taken (rev 3.8):* `baselines/2026-10-12/README.md` — the GUI rows a person owes are listed there, under the launcher rev 3.8 recommends.

**Q01 — `test(storage,daemon): pin the discovery.json bytes, the ConnectionInfo names and the /v1/health JSON`** · Required for Q04–Q06
- The byte pin of §7.2; a `/v1/health` JSON pin in `apps/openalpacad`'s route tests. Gate: the new tests pass on the unchanged code; G-CI. Back-out: revert (tests only).
- *Landed (rev 3.5):* `790bee6c`; the real-file round trip is a writer-built literal, a daemon-written file to join it at E4/E5 (ruling 4).

**Q02 — `build(gui): prepare-sidecar.ts builds through cargo and stages cargo's own artifact; --reuse for development; sha256 and provenance recorded; a GUI-only target dir`** · Recommended (DU-30 a) — before P04 under (w-i)
- `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts` rewritten as §7.2 G2; `package.json`'s `prepare:sidecar:dev|release` scripts; `src-tauri/.gitignore` gains the `.sha256`/`.provenance.json`; `tauri.conf.json`'s `beforeDevCommand` passes `--reuse` when `OPENALPACA_SIDECAR` names a file [NE: the exact mechanism]. Gate: the G2 gate above. Back-out: revert; E5 falls back to the hand sequence.

### 7.4 G4–G6 — measure-first items (no commits scheduled)

- **G4 — streaming render.** Path [C]: each SSE `delta`/`reasoning` frame dispatches (`lib/chat-stream.ts:433-444`); the reducer appends (`:292-298`, `:281-287`); `useChatStream` dispatches into React state (`hooks/useChat.ts:195-214`, `:311-317`); `buildTranscript` is memoized on the whole `stream.state` among eleven inputs (`views/chat/useChatSession.ts:1098-1125`), so every delta rebuilds every item; `Transcript` maps all items (`Transcript.tsx:36-43`); `MessageBody` calls `parseProse(text)` on every render (`components/chat/MessageBody.tsx:117-124`); `UserMessage`/`AssistantMessage` are plain functions (`MessageRow.tsx:84`, `:144`). Change, when justified (rev 3.9: measured on the compiled build, `modernization-plan.md` M69): split the live row out of `buildTranscript`'s inputs; manual memoization only where the compiled build's profile shows work the compiler leaves; only then coalesce deltas to one flush per animation frame with a **mandatory flush** before `done`/`error`/`confirmation_requested` and on unmount, never delaying approval or control events. **Gate first**: a vitest harness replaying a recorded 2,000-delta stream into `useChatSession`, counting `parseProse` calls per history row (expect 1 after; ≈ deltas today) and `MessageRow` renders per row; a React Profiler trace of a 100-message conversation while streaming (commit count, render ms). Worth it = history-row renders per delta ≈ 0 **and** `done.content` and the event order byte-identical in the harness; a lost delta rejects the change.
- **G5 — the latest history page.** Path [C]: `useChatSession.ts:379-406` asks `GET /v1/chat/history?limit=100`, reads `total`, then asks `offset = total − 100`; the route (`routes/chat.rs:657-706`) → `ChatService::get_history` (`chat/service.rs:303-313`) → `list_by_session(limit, offset)` + `count_by_session` (`repository/conversation/mod.rs:197-202`). **The repository already has the tail**: `list_recent_by_session` (`:276-299`, DESC-then-ASC subselect) and `list_recent_by_lane` (`:246-271`), used by the context builder (`orchestrator/context_builder.rs:82-83`, `:248`). So G5 = `tail=true` (or `offset=-1`) on `GET /v1/chat/history` answered by `list_recent_by_session(limit)` with the same `total`, one request for the GUI and for the CLI's `chat --resume` (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/chat.rs:270-324`, the same two calls); `(created_at, id)` is the sort key in both queries, so an append between two requests moves the tail and never duplicates within a page. **Gate first**: the probe→tail latency on a 500-message fixture session (two sequential local requests; the saving is one RTT plus one query, expected small); worth it if §7.5's "first useful content" on a long session improves measurably or the CLI shares the simplification. Tests: route tests for short (< limit), exact (= limit) and long sessions; a concurrent-append test (no missing or duplicate ids across the tail + one older page); `gen_api_docs.py --check`.
- **G6 — lazy previews, then maybe virtualization.** Path [C]: `App.tsx:51-55` lazy-loads the five views; `ChatView.tsx:57` imports `FilePanelSlot` statically, `:64` `WorkPaneSlot`; `FilePanelSlot.tsx:17-35` imports `ArtifactPreview`, `ArtifactDiffTab`, `HistoryTab`, the library preview helpers; `WorkPaneSlot.tsx:19` imports `WorkPane`. The build [A10: `gui-baseline-dist.json`]: `ChatView-*.js` 78,600 B importing `DiffView-*.js` 88,997 B (≈29 KB gzip) and `preview-*.js` 4,831 B; entry 395,009 B. Change: `lazy(() => import("./FilePanelSlot"))` behind the aside's open state with loading/error boundaries, same for `WorkPaneSlot`. **Gate first**: in the packaged webview, the bytes evaluated before first paint of the chat route and the time-to-interactive of the empty chat; worth it if the deferred chunk is not needed for the default screen (the aside is closed by default) **and** first open of a preview stays under a budget the baseline sets (e.g. ≤ 150 ms on the reference machine). **Virtualization only after** a React Profiler + DOM-node count on a 100-message fixture of long code/tables shows layout cost dominating; the acceptance list stands: scroll anchoring while streaming, selection/copy, find, keyboard navigation, approval focus. The 100-message tail and the 500-event ring bound two dimensions, not bytes, DOM nodes or cached sessions [A10].

### 7.5 G7 — the measurement protocol

**Fixture store**: a fresh isolated `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` under `$T` (never the owner's); `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR=<store>/config` with `llm.toml` from `scripts/release/templates/config/llm.toml` plus credential discovery off; the daemon under `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` from its first launch; onboarding finished first (one attended `update_persona` turn; `tasks/lessons.md`); then seeded through the HTTP API: one synthetic registered project (a scratch git repository), one 100-message conversation of long text/code/tables, one 20-note memory partition, five artifacts (markdown, image, a diff pair, …). Snapshot the store once and copy it for every trial.

**Processes to count** [Docs/NE — nothing was launched]: the shell (`openalpaca_gui` on macOS) and its WebKit XPC helpers (`com.apple.WebKit.WebContent`, `.Networking`, `.GPU`, attributed as "responsible process"); `openalpacad` and its children (MCP servers, plugins — none in the fixture). Windows: `openalpaca-gui.exe` + the `msedgewebview2.exe` tree; Linux: the app + `WebKitWebProcess`/`WebKitNetworkProcess`. Per process **and** a combined figure using the OS's non-double-counting measure — macOS `footprint`/`vmmap -summary` (phys_footprint), Linux PSS from `/proc/<pid>/smaps_rollup`, Windows private working set — never a sum of RSS across processes sharing pages. Local-model memory (Ollama) reported separately and excluded.

**Scenarios** (5 cold trials each; median and p95; the driver's OS clock): (1) cold launch, no daemon → first shell paint, first useful content (the transcript rendered), daemon-ready (`/v1/health` 200); (2) launch with the daemon running; (3) idle foreground 60 s and hidden/minimized 60 s → memory and CPU %; (4) one streamed 2,000-token answer → peak memory, dropped frames; (5) the 100-message conversation opened and scrolled; (6) three previews opened; (7) ten window close/reopen cycles with the daemon surviving (same `instance_id`; closing never stops it); (8) build-side: clean full build, warm full build, shell-only incremental (`touch src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), support-only incremental, daemon-only incremental, React HMR turnaround — each with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, profile, toolchain, hardware and cache state recorded.

**Rules**: baseline on the same application commit before each architecture step; compare like with like (release package vs release package); **set budgets from the baseline** — this plan writes no MB/ms target.

### 7.6 G8 — the native CI matrix

Today `ci.yml` excludes `openalpaca_gui` from Rust (`:27-31`), the `gui` job runs the four bun gates on ubuntu (`:37-58`), the only Windows job is Pester (`:64-78`) [C].

| Job | Runner | Steps | Sidecar | Wall-clock [NE, reasoned] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `shell-check` ×3 (Q08) | `macos-latest`, `windows-latest`, `ubuntu-latest` (+ `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev` [Docs: v2.tauri.app/start/prerequisites]) | `bun run build` (codegen needs `dist/`), `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked`, `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`; after Q06 `cargo test -p openalpaca_app_support` | an **empty placeholder** at `src-tauri/bin/openalpacad-<triple>` created by the job in the fresh checkout and deleted at the end (tauri-build errors when it is absent [D]); never where a real sidecar exists | cold 10–20 min (≈245 crates); warm 2–5 min |
| `native-smoke` ×3 (Q09) | the same three | `cargo build --release -p openalpacad --locked`, `prepare-sidecar.ts --release`, `tauri build` (`--bundles app` / `msi` / `appimage`), then the packaged app against an isolated store: launch → `/v1/health` → `instance_id` stable across close/reopen → explicit stop + the lifecycle wait → `daemon.log` tail after a failing start (`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` unwritable) → Reveal on a store file and a project file, refusals on a missing project and a symlink escape (through `reveal_path`, by a test page or `tauri-driver`; WebDriver on macOS is limited, so that job may assert launch/reuse/stop only [Docs]) | the real daemon | cold 25–45 min per OS (the 364-crate release build); warm 8–15 min |

Cost: macOS minutes bill 10× and Windows 2× on a private repository, free on a public one [Docs: GitHub Actions billing]; the repository's visibility is [NE]. Reasoned: `shell-check` ≈ 33 billed-minute-equivalents warm per PR on a private repo; `native-smoke` ≈ 490 cold / ≈ 150 warm — too much per PR, hence nightly and on tags. Gaps labelled per OS: WKWebView rendering is asserted by none of it; dialogs are not driveable headless.

### 7.7 Ordering with Part I

| Part II step | Depends on Part I? | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Q00 baselines | **no — and must precede both parts** | every comparison needs a same-architecture, same-dependency baseline; taken at the frozen application commit before P01 |
| Q01 pins, Q02/Q03 provenance | no | script and test work; Q02/Q03 make G-ident/E5/E7 mechanical, so land them **before P04** (DU-30 w-i) |
| Q04 contract | no | additive fields, independent of any bump |
| Q05/Q06 extraction | no for correctness; **yes for comparisons**: after Q06 the shell's dependency graph changes (storage leaves; sysinfo/chrono/file-lock arrive through support), so G-GUI for P04/P08/P31a is run against the architecture they land on and the shell baseline is **recaptured** after Q06 | P06's rusqlite stops reaching the shell after Q06; P08's sysinfo still does |
| Q07 HostBridge | no | TS only; one fixture host instead of 23 mocks for P31–P33's re-runs |
| Q08 shell-check | depends on P01 (SHA pins, `rust-toolchain.toml`), DU-22 | supersedes P01a/P40 |
| Q09 native-smoke | depends on Q02/Q03 and P01 | — |
| G4–G6 | no | need Q00's frontend profile first |

**Recommended order** (rev 3.9): Q00 (taken) → P02 → Q01 → Q02 → Q03 (landed) → Q15 → Q16 → Q17 → the G-base driver amended (§5.1 item 6b) → **Part I in its phase order: P01, P01b (landed) → P42 → P03 → P31a (the Tauri pair) → Q11 → P04 … P38, with P28 → P41 in Phase H; the security-bearing P04, P15, P16 as early as the order allows** → Q12 → Q13 → Q14 → Q04 → Q05 → Q06 → recapture the shell baseline (G-GUI, `cargo tree`) → Q07 → Q08 (dropping P01a/P40 if they are still open) → Q09 → Q18 (once the owner states the floor) → Q10 → G4–G6 as Q10's profiles justify → E5/E7/E8 with the §5 identity check. `modernization-plan.md`'s Part III follows P38 (its rev 2.0 §6.3); its shell commits M72–M75 may land after P31a and Q11. Dependency groups are always compared against a baseline of the same architecture. **The alternative** (the architect's): Q04–Q06 before Part I, so the GUI's dependency bumps never reach the shell; it costs a re-baseline of the shell mid-wave and delays the five advisories by the extraction's review time — not recommended while lopdf 0.34.0 and rustls 0.23.43 are in the lock.

### 7.8 What Part II explicitly does not do

- No framework migration: not Electrobun, Wails, Electron; not Tauri 3 (alpha.4 only) and not CEF (a Chromium distribution against the packaging goal); the system webview stays, its rendering differences tested per OS.
- No RAM, startup or compile-time promise: every such claim is [NE] until Q00's baseline and Q10's recapture exist; crate counts are not runtime figures.
- No new reader of `project.json` in the shell: Reveal keeps the one canonical reader (moved, not copied); webview-supplied roots are never authority.
- No token in a bundle, no loosening of CORS or path checks for browser development.
- No change to storage formats, migrations or the daemon's API beyond the additive `protocol` field; `test-home-guard` behaviour is not lost in the move.
- No G4–G6 commit before its gate.

## 9. Keeping current afterwards

Rev 2.1 §8 stands (monthly Dependabot with a 7-day cooldown; the weekly advisories jobs; semver-major bumps hand-planned one commit per crate with the format pin first; the toolchain moved deliberately every second or third stable, an exact x.y.z, `rust-version` with it; Bun bumped with `packageManager` and the local install together), with these changes:

- **Dependabot's bun updater is at Bun 1.4.2 and accepts `bun.lock` versions ≤ 3** (source level, since 2026-10-06) [Docs]. Under DU-13 (a) nothing watches the lock version any more; the first hosted run on the v2 lock is the one check (P35's gate). Under (b) the §8 checklist keeps watching `lockfileVersion`.
- **The Tauri pairing rule, three guards** (rev 3.9): (1) the requirement strings in `[workspace.dependencies]` — `tauri = "~2.12"` and `tauri-build = "~2.7"` to the minor (tilde — rev 3.10 corrected rev 3.9's caret, which admits the next minor), `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=…"` exactly (P31a) — stop a lockfile-only cargo PR or a plain `cargo update` from moving a Tauri minor or a plugin patch alone; (2) Dependabot's `ignore` for `tauri*`/`@tauri-apps/*` minors and for **every** update type of `tauri-plugin-*`/`@tauri-apps/plugin-*` (DU-15 (p-i), widened); (3) G-GUI and G-bundle on every pair commit. (p-ii) is withdrawn. A Tauri minor or a plugin patch is always a hand-made commit that moves both locks (`tauri` ↔ `@tauri-apps/api` same minor; each `tauri-plugin-*` = its `@tauri-apps/plugin-*` exactly; a crate-only patch, as 2.0.3–2.0.5 were, a Rust-only commit that says so), followed by an E5-style packaged check. The Tauri CLI's own check compares nothing under Bun [M-today] — an upstream tauri-cli issue is the way out (the owner's to file).
- **The per-PR checklist** (every dependency PR, Dependabot's included): (1) the data-path watch-list (P04) — read the changelog of every listed crate the PR moves; (2) G-GUI by hand (or Q08's `shell-check`) when the PR moves anything the shell links — `tauri*`, `wry`, `tao`, `windows*`, `sysinfo`, and until Q06 `openalpaca_storage`'s dependencies; (3) the format-pinning tests ran and passed: P05's AES fixture, the `sha256_hex("abc")` and the UUID partition-key literal, the owner-key and folder-suffix pins, the newer-than-build refusal (`database/tests.rs:68`), the serde-saphyr pin (P30), P14's characterisations, Q01's discovery byte pin — a failing pin is a format change, never a test to update in passing; (4) the Tauri pairing (above); (5) a packaged build gets G-ident — bundled == staged `.sha256` == `libexec/` (Q02/Q03), markers from the lock; (6) a `bun.lock` PR keeps the committed `lockfileVersion` (DU-13).
- **Freshness.** A hand-planned bump takes the newest release ≥ 7 days old in both ecosystems. **Cargo 1.100 (2026-11-12) stabilises `registry.global-min-publish-age`** — the resolver form of this rule; adopt it in `.cargo/config.toml` once the pin is ≥ 1.100 (then P04-style `--precise` step-backs are no longer hand work). Cargo 1.100 also changes the intermediate build-directory layout (Q02 reads cargo's JSON messages, so it is unaffected; anything else reading `target/<profile>` is) and makes `cargo install --locked` a no-op [Docs: the Cargo Book changelog].
- **Rev 3.9:** the freshness rule is applied at the cut by DU-23's minute table; a step-back is taken only for a release younger than 7 days, never to keep a measured older version (DU-25 (v4) took rmcp 3.5.1 on this rule). `build.build-dir` (stable since 1.91) is adopted by `modernization-plan.md` M63, with the shell's build-dir equal to its target-dir because tauri-build resolves its sidecar copy from `OUT_DIR`.
- **The weekly advisories run** (P34): a red run on an unchanged lock is a new advisory — a lock refresh, a bump, or a reasoned `ignore` (`deny.toml`) / `--ignore=<CVE>` (`bun audit`). The one pre-recorded candidate: zip2's three `extract()`-path GHSAs against zip 8.6.0, lifted when calamine moves to zip 9. Once a year, read the transitive unmaintained list (4 warnings after the wave).
- **The Rust toolchain**: the procedure is P03's gate (clippy and fmt counts by the stated method against the previous toolchain, the full suite, G-GUI, the future-incompat list; the lock refresh after it). 1.100.0 is eligible from 2026-11-19.
- **Bun and Node**: `packageManager` bumped with the local Bun (exactly that version); 1.4.3 is taken at the cut (P42, rev 3.9; its own one-line commit with the four gates re-run). Node stays with the runner image until a floor is threatened (today 22.23.3 default on 24.04, 24.21.0 on 26.04).
- **Part II's own upkeep**: after any architecture step the shell's dependency baseline is recaptured before the next dependency comparison; `openalpaca_app_support` joins the guard audit; the `shell-check` jobs are the standing G-GUI; `native-smoke`'s labelled gaps are re-read when a platform tool (WebDriver on macOS) improves.
- **Owner settings, once**: Dependabot alerts on; local Bun at the pinned version.
